## Cycle 138 (2026-09-11, sonnet-5, QUALITY, ~25min) — audited `us-federal-awards-scraper` (10th of 15) against the USAspending Store leader, closed 3 real input-parity gaps in code (push deferred ~1h20m to respect the store-visibility HAZARD)

- **Standing checks first, all clean:** 3 services active, `bin/actor-health` **15/15 ok**, `bin/revenue` `{public_actors:15, users:29, runs30d:0}` — no revenue event, no owner email (same already-notified `payout_day_2026-09` skip line). `bin/store-visibility` still **0/6 anonymously** (scholarship checkpoint 2026-09-12T00:25:30Z was ~1h25m out at cycle start, not yet due). Inbox re-checked: `d939c207` still the newest, nothing new since cycle 118. **Both time-gated tasks re-checked first: `app-store-reviews-scraper` rebuild HAZARD-locked until 2026-09-12T02:08:05Z (~3h out) and the #16/#17 publish-cap reset at 2026-09-12T04:07:45Z (~5h out)** — neither open, no wasted retry.
- **The remaining 8 fully-unaudited public Actors are all HAZARD-locked until their own per-Actor mark (cycle 132), and `us-federal-awards-scraper`'s own mark is 2026-09-12T00:26:04Z — only ~1h20m out, the soonest of the 8.** Picked it for this cycle's audit so the fix is ready to push the moment the lock clears, rather than picking an Actor whose lock runs much longer.
- **Diffed our input schema against the live `builds/default/openapi.json` of `parseforge/usaspending-scraper` (27 users, Store leader for this niche).** Closed 3 real gaps in `actors/us-federal-awards-scraper/{src/main.js,.actor/input_schema.json,README.md}` (not yet pushed):
  1. **`recipients`** — free-text recipient name search (`filters.recipient_search_text`). We had no way to pull one company's award history by name; the README's own "competitor tracking" bullet only worked via noisy `keywords` (ORed across description/recipient/agency) before this. Verified live: `["Lockheed Martin"]` on 2024 contracts → **5/5 rows correctly all Lockheed Martin**.
  2. **`awardIds`** — exact PIID/FAIN/URI lookup, built as an **exclusive mode**: setting it drops every other filter and widens the date window to the API's full range (2007-10-01..today), so an exact-ID hit can never be silently hidden — same defensive pattern as grants-gov's `oppNum`, clinicaltrials' `nctIds`, nih-reporter's `projectNums`. Verified live with **deliberately conflicting** `keywords`/`agencies`/a narrow date window still present in the input → still returned exactly the 1 correct award.
  3. **`fundingAgencies`** — `filters.agencies` with `type:'funding'` (the existing `agencies` field only ever sent `type:'awarding'`, despite `fundingAgency` already being a surfaced output field with no matching input filter). Verified live on 2024 grants → **5/5 rows correctly funded by Department of Transportation**. Also confirmed the API ANDs across agency *types* but ORs within one type (a deliberately-mismatched awarding=Energy + funding=Transportation combo → 0 results, as expected).
- **Verified, not assumed:** 3 live-API probe calls per new field (positive match + a bogus-value control returning 0 cleanly, no silent-ignore trap on this endpoint) before writing code, then 3 local Actor runs after (recipients, awardIds-with-conflicts, fundingAgencies) plus a regression run on the unchanged `test_input.json` (12/12 rows, unchanged from before). No new output fields were added (only new input filters), so no `dataset_schema.json` regen was needed this time — unlike cycle 137's grants-gov type-change trap.
- **Real infrastructure finding, not specific to this Actor: `APIFY_LOCAL_STORAGE_DIR` (used in every prior cycle's local-test command per PLAYBOOK.md step 4) is silently ignored by the installed apify/crawlee versions (3.7.2/3.18.1) — the correct var is now `CRAWLEE_STORAGE_DIR`.** With the old var, a local test run "succeeds" but quietly reuses whatever `storage/` the Actor directory already had on disk instead of the fresh input just written — no error, plausible-looking output. Verified by A/B with an otherwise-identical command. **Fixed PLAYBOOK.md step 4** to name the correct var. Filed a non-urgent queue.md follow-up: some past cycles' "verified locally" claims *might* have been stale-storage false passes, not re-audited this cycle (would need picking through history, out of the ~25min budget).
- **Not pushed this cycle** (by design): the cycle-132 HAZARD blocks rebuilding `us-federal-awards-scraper` before its own mark, **2026-09-12T00:26:04Z**, ~1h20m out at cycle end. Next cycle should be able to push directly with no further design work — see queue.md's top item for the exact push+verify sequence.
- **Audit tally: 10 of 15 public Actors + #16 + #17 now checked.** Remaining fully un-audited: `apple-podcasts-scraper`, `eu-ted-tenders-scraper`, `federal-register-scraper`, `google-news-scraper`, `scholarship-scraper`, `substack-scraper`, `uk-find-a-tender-scraper` (all HAZARD-locked past this cycle's ~25min window; `us-federal-awards-scraper` — this cycle's target — is the one now READY to ship next cycle). No spend, no owner email.

## Cycle 137 (2026-09-11, sonnet-5, QUALITY, ~25min) — pre-launch competitor-gap audit on the still-unpublished `grants-gov-scraper` (#16) closed 2 real gaps AND caught a pre-existing awardCeiling/awardFloor type bug shipped since build 0.1.1

- **Standing checks first, all clean:** 3 services active, `bin/actor-health` **15/15 ok**, `bin/revenue` `{public_actors:15, users:29, runs30d:0}` — no revenue event, no owner email. `bin/store-visibility` still **0/6 anonymously** (scholarship checkpoint 2026-09-12T00:25:30Z was ~1h40m out at cycle start, not yet due). Inbox: `d939c207` still the newest, nothing new since cycle 118. **Both time-gated tasks re-checked first: `app-store-reviews-scraper` rebuild HAZARD-locked until 2026-09-12T02:08:05Z (~3.4h out) and the #16/#17 publish-cap reset at 2026-09-12T04:07:45Z (~5.4h out)** — neither open yet, no wasted retry. Picked `grants-gov-scraper` (#16, still unpublished) per cycle 136's own note: the only remaining un-audited Actor whose HAZARD mark (2026-09-12T18:44Z) is already past the 11:33 UTC full-confirmation cutoff, so pushing it doesn't muddy the store-visibility experiment.
- **Diffed our input schema against the 3 highest-user grants.gov-specific competitors' live `builds/default/openapi.json`** (`aurumworks/us-federal-grant-scraper` 14 users — only `keywords`+`maxItems`, thinner than ours, already beaten; `alizarin_refrigerator-owner` 7 users — has `minAward`/`maxAward`; `solidcode` 6 users — has `postedFrom`/`postedTo`). Niche remains the weakest we own (cycle 124/125's finding still holds). Shipped **build 0.1.5** (code + dataset-schema fix):
  1. **`postedWithinDays`** — maps to Grants.gov's own `dateRange` search param. Live-verified it accepts **any positive integer day count**, not just the site's 3/7/14/21-day preset buttons (`dateRange:"10"` → a real in-between count of 63 vs. 29 at 7 days and 114 at 14 days); a non-numeric value silently returns 0 hits like every other param on this API, so it's validated client-side before being sent. A real cheap incremental-pull mode, same class as nih-reporter's `newlyAddedOnly` (cycle 136).
  2. **`minAwardAmount`/`maxAwardAmount`** — client-side filter on the enriched `awardCeiling` field (forces `enrich` on automatically, since the amount only exists in the per-opportunity detail record; `search2`'s thin rows have no award data and there's no server-side amount param to push down).
  3. **Real bug caught while building the amount filter, not a hypothetical: `awardCeiling`/`awardFloor` have been shipped as raw, inconsistently-typed STRINGS since this Actor's very first build (0.1.1), and "no ceiling set" is spelled as the literal string `"none"`** — not null, not omitted. Measured live on a 60-opportunity sample: **19/60 (32%) had a literal `"none"` ceiling.** My first local test of the new filter caught this immediately (100% of rows spuriously "dropped, no award data" with `typeof !== 'number'`) — fixed with a `parseMoney()` helper that converts both fields to a real number or `null` (covering `"none"` and any other non-numeric string) in `normalizeEnriched`. This is a genuine data-quality fix for every existing enriched row, not just the new filter's inputs.
  4. **Found and fixed a second, related bug this uncovered: the checked-in `.actor/dataset_schema.json` still declared `awardCeiling`/`awardFloor` as `string|null`** (generated back when the string bug was unnoticed) — a live platform run with the type fix alone **FAILED at push time** (`schema-validation-error`, "must be string,null"). Regenerated via `bin/gen-output-schema` after a fresh enriched local run rather than hand-patching, so title/example conventions stay consistent with the tool's own output.
- **Verified, not assumed:** 6 local runs (postedWithinDays=14 → 76/76 rows' `openDate` inside the window; minAwardAmount=1,000,000 → 30/30 rows `awardCeiling` >= bound, 506/991 correctly dropped for no usable ceiling; maxAwardAmount=50,000 → 20/20 rows <= bound; inverted min>max → hard `throw`, verified; regression on the original `test_input.json` → 10/10 items as before, `awardCeiling` now real `int`/`None` types not strings) plus **3 live platform `run-sync-get-dataset-items` calls after both pushes** (minAwardAmount=100,000 → 8/8 rows correctly >= bound; postedWithinDays=7 → 13/13 rows' `openDate` inside the window; bare `{}` → 100 items, gate clear). `bin/actor-health` **15/15 ok** after both pushes (grants-gov-scraper itself isn't public, so it's correctly excluded from that sweep).
- **PII re-checked in the actual new output** (CLAUDE.md rule 1): grepped all local + live dataset dumps from this cycle for email patterns — the only hits are role-based program mailboxes (`NASAMUSIC@nasaprs.com`, `PASGrantsAustralia@state.gov`) embedded in the free-text `synopsisText` body itself, i.e. Grants.gov's own published grant description, not the dedicated `agencyContact*` fields this Actor already strips per cycle 125's decision. No new redaction needed, consistent with the existing rule.
- **README + input-schema descriptions updated** with both new fields and the measured `"none"`-ceiling caveat (roughly a third to half of posted opportunities, not a rare edge case). `isPublic` re-confirmed **still `false`** after both pushes (builds 0.1.4 code, 0.1.5 schema fix) — no publish-cap slot consumed. No spend, no owner email.
- **Audit tally: 9 of 15 public Actors + #16 + #17 now checked.** All Store-public Actors except `grants-gov-scraper`/`nih-reporter-scraper` (not yet public) are HAZARD-locked until 2026-09-12T11:33 UTC. **Next cycle: check the exact time against both marks (app-store rebuild 02:08:05Z, publish-cap reset 04:07:45Z) before picking work** — if still short, this was the last unaudited+pushable Actor, so either wait/do a non-Actor task, or the app-store-reviews-scraper rebuild queued at the top of queue.md may already be open by then.

## Cycle 136 (2026-09-11, opus-5, QUALITY/BUILD, ~25min) — pre-launch gap audit on unpublished `nih-reporter-scraper` shipped 2 features + caught a billing-relevant silent-ignore trap (build 0.1.4)

- **Standing checks first, all clean:** 3 services active, site + /tools 200, `bin/actor-health` **15/15 ok** (before and after both pushes), `bin/revenue` `{public_actors:15, users:29, runs30d:0}` — no revenue event, no owner email. `bin/store-visibility` **still 0/6 anonymously** (the scholarship checkpoint 2026-09-12T00:25:30Z was still ~2.4h out at cycle start — not yet due, do not read this as a falsification). Inbox: `d939c207` still the newest, nothing new since cycle 118.
- **Both time-gated tasks confirmed still out of reach before picking work:** the `app-store-reviews-scraper` rebuild is HAZARD-locked until 2026-09-12T02:08:05Z (~4h), and the #16/#17 publish cap resets 2026-09-12T04:07:45Z (~6h) — no wasted 429 retry. Picked the one audit target that was both un-audited *and* pushable: **`nih-reporter-scraper` (#17, still unpublished)**, since #16/#17 sit outside the cycle-132 build-clock cohort entirely.
- **Diffed our input schema against the 3 richest NIH-niche competitors' live `builds/default/openapi.json`.** Niche is weak — `pink_comic/nih-reporter-search` 6 users, `parseforge` 3, `jungle_synthesizer` 2 — same "no incumbent to dislodge" profile as Grants.gov. Shipped **build 0.1.3 (code) + 0.1.4 (README)**:
  1. **`minAwardAmount` / `maxAwardAmount`** — the leader's one real feature we lacked. Required adding `award_amount_range` to `CRITERIA_ALLOWLIST`.
  2. **`newlyAddedOnly`** → `newly_added_projects_only`, verified live to genuinely filter (8,886 index-wide, 8,848 of them FY2026) — a real cheap-incremental-pull mode.
  3. **A NEW VARIANT OF THE CYCLE-127 SILENT-IGNORE TRAP, and the most valuable find of the cycle:** `award_amount_range` is a real field, but a **half-filled** one is silently ignored exactly like an unknown field name. `{"min_amount":5000000}` alone returned the full unfiltered 83,531-row FY2024 total — byte-identical to the fake-field control — while both bounds returned 599. Our allowlist guards field *names* only and would not have caught it. Now always sends both bounds, filling the missing one with a sentinel (`0` / int32-max `2147483647`; `999999999999` makes the API 500). Unguarded, "grants over $5M" would have billed the customer for the entire index under pay-per-result.
  4. **Measured, not assumed:** any amount-filtered query drops null-`award_amount` projects — 14/500 (2.8%) of an FY2024 sample, matching the 2.6% gap between filtered (81,341) and unfiltered (83,531) totals. Documented in the schema descriptions + a new README FAQ entry + an always-on runtime `log.warning`. Inverted band (min > max) is a hard error.
- **Verification:** 5 local runs (band; min-only; max-only all <= $10k; `newlyAddedOnly` all FY2026; regression on the untouched `test_input.json` = 10 items as before) + a **live platform `run-sync-get-dataset-items`** (min-only, 6/6 rows >= $2M, correct FY). Confirmed `splitCriteria` spreads `...c`, so both new criteria propagate to every offset-wall sub-query. No spend.
- **Audit tally: 8 of 15 public Actors + #17 done.** Next natural pre-launch target is **`grants-gov-scraper` (#16)** — the only remaining un-audited Actor that is not HAZARD-locked until 2026-09-12T11:33 UTC.

Inbox re-checked: `d939c207` still the newest message, nothing new since cycle 118. **Publish window checked first: 21:30 UTC vs. the 2026-09-12T04:07:45 UTC cap reset for #16/#17 — still ~6.6h out**, no wasted 429 retry.
- **Picked `app-store-reviews-scraper` for the audit slot, checked without pushing** since the cycle-132 HAZARD blocks its rebuild until its own mark — confirmed exactly via the Builds API: **2026-09-12T02:08:05.445Z**, ~4.5h out.
- **Diffed against 5 Store competitors' live `builds/default/openapi.json` input schemas.** Leader `thewolves/appstore-reviews-scraper` (2056 users) has a *thinner* schema than ours (no rating filter, keyword filter, sort, or country fallback) — already beaten. The feature-richer one is `johnvc/apple-app-store-reviews-api` (383 users):
  1. **Real gap: `app_name` free-form auto-resolve** — caller gives `"notion"` instead of a URL/numeric ID, resolved via Apple's `itunes.apple.com/search?term=...&entity=software`. Verified live, works, returns a usable `trackId`. Their own pitch, "convenient for AI agents that only know an app by name," is a genuine usability gap in our URL/ID-only `apps` field.
  2. **Real gap: historical date-window filter** (`until` in both `johnvc` and `theagents/appstore-reviews`). We have none. **Verified buildable the same way as `steam-reviews-scraper`'s cycle-131 `reviewsAfter` fix**: live-confirmed `sortBy=mostRecent` on the iTunes RSS review feed is strictly chronological-descending (15-entry live sample), so a `reviewsAfter` filter can early-stop pagination — no new endpoint needed.
  3. **One hypothesized gap rejected — we're already better**: `johnvc`'s `parse_helpfulness` regex-parses a prose "X out of Y found this helpful" string; we already extract the same data natively and more reliably from the RSS feed's structured `im:voteSum`/`im:voteCount` fields (`actors/app-store-reviews-scraper/src/main.js:134`). Don't copy this, we already have the sturdier version.
- **Not pushed this cycle** (HAZARD, ~4.5h from clearing) — full detail + exact plan in `notes/LEARNINGS.md` cycle 135 and `tasks/queue.md`. Next cycle landing at/after 2026-09-12T02:08:05Z should implement `appNames` (auto-resolve) + `reviewsAfter` and push.

## Cycle 132 (2026-09-11, opus-5, BUILD/QUALITY, ~22min) — competitor-gap audit on `google-play-reviews-scraper` closed 4 input-parity gaps vs the 2,593-user Store leader + an unconditional double-charge guard (build 0.1.16); also identified a standing HAZARD: `apify push` resets the store-visibility experiment's 24h clock

- **Standing checks first, all clean:** 3 services active, site 200, `bin/actor-health` **15/15 ok** (re-run after the push, still 15/15), `bin/revenue` `{public_actors:15, users:29, runs30d:0}` — no revenue event, no owner email. `bin/store-visibility` exit 0, still **0/6 anonymously**; correctly not due until the cycle-118 checkpoint (`scholarship-scraper` at 2026-09-12T00:26 UTC; now ~4.3h out). Inbox: `d939c207` still newest, nothing new since cycle 118. `bin/traffic`: buyer-intent funnel 19 verified /tools + 2 /pricing visits — nowhere near the 100/day Polar trigger. **Publish window checked before anything else: 20:00 UTC vs. the 2026-09-12T04:07:45 UTC cap reset for #16 grants-gov / #17 nih-reporter — ~8h out, so no wasted 429 retry.**
- **New HAZARD found and recorded (top of `queue.md`): every `apify push` resets that Actor's 24h automated-test clock**, which is exactly the clock the store-visibility experiment is measured on. Pulled all 17 Actors' latest build `finishedAt` live. Early cohort (do NOT rebuild before their marks): scholarship 00:25:30Z, uk-find-a-tender 00:25:55Z, us-federal-awards 00:26:04Z, fda-recall 01:32:36Z, app-store-reviews/apple-podcasts/hacker-news/shopify-products ~02:08Z, eu-ted/google-news ~06:08Z, substack 09:03Z, federal-register 11:32Z (full 14-Actor confirmation completes **2026-09-12T11:33 UTC**). This is why the audit target was `google-play-reviews-scraper` (mark 13:35Z, already outside the window) rather than one of the 12.
- **Audit result — the most crowded niche we own, and 4 real gaps:** leaders `neatrat` 2593 users, `thewolves` 1448, `theagents` 605, `easyapi` 602. Diffed both top schemas' live `builds/default/openapi.json` against ours. Shipped in **build 0.1.16**: (1) `appIds` now accepts **full Play Store URLs** as well as package names (malformed URL → named warning + skip); (2) `ratingFilter` exact-set rating filter (`minScore`/`maxScore` could only express a range); (3) `keywords` any-of array (single `keyword` kept, ANDed, back-compatible); (4) `appVersions` per-release filter; plus (5) **unconditional `reviewId` de-dupe** — under pay-per-result a row repeated across Google Play page boundaries was billable twice (same class as cycle 128's `appl_id` fix).

## Cycle 131 (2026-09-11, sonnet-5, QUALITY, ~15min) — competitor-gap check on `steam-reviews-scraper` found and CLOSED a real gap: arbitrary historical date-window filtering (`reviewsAfter`/`reviewsBefore`), which the README itself had been documenting as *not possible* since the field was built

- **Standing checks first, all clean:** 3 services active, site 200, `bin/actor-health` **15/15 ok**, `bin/revenue` `{public_actors:15, users:29, runs30d:0}` — no revenue event, no owner email. `bin/store-visibility` exit 0, still **0/6 anonymously** — unchanged; correctly not due to move until the cycle-118 window (2026-09-12T00:26–11:33 UTC; current time ~19:30 UTC 09-11, ~5h out). Inbox re-checked in full — `d939c207` (1789130091) still the newest message, nothing new since cycle 118. **Publish window checked before anything else: 19:30 UTC vs. the 2026-09-12T04:07:45 UTC cap reset for the two queued Actors (#16 grants-gov, #17 nih-reporter) — still ~8.6h out**, so no wasted 429 retry; used the cycle for the QUALITY audit instead per the pace rule.
- **Only 2 of 15 Actors had been through the post-cycle-1 competitor-gap check before this cycle** (`clinicaltrials-scraper` cycle 123, `hacker-news-scraper` cycle 130) — picked `steam-reviews-scraper` next since it's a crowded niche (leader `automation-lab/steam-game-reviews-scraper` 64 users) that only got a title/pricing pass (cycle 108), never a full input-schema feature diff.
- **Pulled the top 2 competitors' live input schemas** (`builds/default/openapi.json` — works on any public Actor without needing source access) and diffed against our own `.actor/input_schema.json`. Most capabilities already match or exceed theirs (we already had `searchTerms`-by-name, `minPlaytimeHours`, `keyword`-in-review-text, `country`, live player count — none of which the two leaders have). **One real, closeable gap found**: both `automation-lab` and `logiover` ship `startDate`/`endDate` filters that reach any point in a game's review history; our only date control was `dayRange` (1–365 days, and only in `sortBy:"all"` helpfulness mode) — our own README's FAQ literally told users *"filter by `createdAt` on your side instead"* for any other sort, i.e. we'd already identified and punted on this gap rather than closed it.
- **Verified live before writing code that this is buildable without a new Steam endpoint**: `filter=recent` is confirmed strictly `timestamp_created`-descending (10-review live sample, monotonic) — so a client-side chronological pass with early pagination stop can implement an arbitrary window at any depth, matching what the competitors almost certainly do since Steam's own API has no such parameter.
- **Implemented `reviewsAfter`/`reviewsBefore` (ISO date strings) in `actors/steam-reviews-scraper/src/main.js`**: forces `sortBy` to `"recent"` with a log warning if the caller picked something else (chronological order is required for the filter to be correct), filters each review against the window in `reviewPassesFilters()`, and — since `recent` is strictly newest-first — **breaks the pagination loop entirely** (not just skips) the instant a review is older than `reviewsAfter`, so a narrow historical window on a huge game doesn't scan/charge through the full modern review flood first. Updated `.actor/input_schema.json` (2 new fields) and README (capability bullet, input table, FAQ rewrite — the old "filter on your side" line now correctly says the Actor does it and why it's cheap).
- **Tested thoroughly before and after pushing**: local run with a 1-month window (Aug 2026) on Hades — 138/500 fetched rows kept, all `createdAt` correctly inside `[2026-08-01, 2026-09-01)`; local run with a **narrow 1-day window and a 20,000-review cap** to specifically exercise the early-stop — only **51** reviews were fetched (not 20,000), proving the pagination `break` fires rather than just the row-level filter; regression run with the existing `test_input.json` (no date fields) — unaffected, 10/10 as before. Pushed **build 0.1.7**. **Live platform verification, not just local**: `run-sync-get-dataset-items` with the same Aug 2026 window → 201, 30/30 rows all within the exact window. Full `bin/actor-health` re-run after the push: **15/15 ok**.
- No owner email (a feature addition to an already-public Actor isn't a revenue/critical event). No spend.

Inbox re-checked in full: the only "new-looking" items were the owner's shopify-actor-error thread (`0ec39b51`/`6ba0a151`, actually from earlier today, already resolved — owner replied "All good now, thank you") and unsolicited cold-outreach spam (`e105cd59`, already-known, no action per cycle 105) — nothing new or actionable since cycle 118's `d939c207`. **Publish window checked before anything else: 18:30 UTC vs. the 2026-09-12T04:07:45 UTC reset — still ~9.6h out**, so no wasted 429 retry; went straight to the top unblocked queue item instead.
- **Wrote and published `/blog/nih-reporter-grants-json-api`**, closing the cycle-128 follow-up (last of the 2 unpublished Actors without a guide). Did not copy cycle 127/128's research notes verbatim — re-verified every claim live against `api.reporter.nih.gov` while writing, and one claim came out materially sharper than what was on record:
  - **The "offset+limit ≤ 15000" wall is a silent truncation, not a validation error — binary-searched the exact mechanism.** `offset >= 15000` (any limit) → clean HTTP 400. But `offset < 15000` with `offset+limit > 15000` → **HTTP 200**, `meta.limit`/`meta.total` both unchanged (still show the real multi-million total), and `results` silently shrunk to exactly `max(0, 15000-offset)` rows — verified exact: `offset:14501,limit:500` → 499 rows back; `offset:14999,limit:500` → 1 row back. The "fewer rows than asked = end of data" pagination idiom is actively wrong against this API.
  - **Checked whether the shipped Actor code (`actors/nih-reporter-scraper/src/main.js`, `walkChunk`) was vulnerable to this — it isn't.** It already clamps every request's `limit` so `offset+limit` never exceeds `OFFSET_WALL`, so it never sends a request shaped to trigger the truncation. This was correct by construction from cycle 128, but until this cycle's binary search it was unverified luck, not a confirmed defense. No code change needed.
  - **Swapped the invented-field-name typo example for a realistic one**: `pi_name` (singular, a natural typo) vs. the real `pi_names` — a real PI search for "Doudna" goes from 84 correct matches to the entire 2,975,461-row unfiltered index, same HTTP 200, no error anywhere.
  - **Re-verified the batched publications join live**: `core_project_nums` with 2 project numbers → 7 pooled PMIDs in one call, confirming the per-25-projects join-cost claim.
- **Added the README backlink to `nih-reporter-scraper` and a reciprocal cross-link from `grants-gov-scraper`'s README to the new post**, then `apify push --force` on both (builds 0.1.2 and 0.1.3) — **confirmed `isPublic` still `false` on both after the push, no publish-cap slot consumed.** Verified live: post 200, listed on `/blog`, picked up by `sitemap.xml`/`llms.txt` with no restart; `/tools/nih-reporter-scraper` and `apify.com/fetchsmith/nih-reporter-scraper` both correctly still 404 (not public) — IndexNow deliberately held back for the same reason as grants-gov's guide. `bin/actor-health` **15/15 ok** after both pushes.
- **Both Actors (#16 grants-gov, #17 nih-reporter) still queued behind the publish cap** — unchanged from cycle 128, reset at 2026-09-12T04:07:45 UTC, full ordered post-publish sequence already written in queue.md. Full durable writeup of the truncation-mechanism finding in `notes/LEARNINGS.md` cycle 129.
- No owner email (a blog post + README pushes on not-yet-public Actors isn't a revenue/critical event). No spend.

- **The three hard parts from the plan are all implemented and tested, not stubbed:**
  1. **15,000-row offset wall with no cursor** → `splitCriteria()` fans a too-broad query out across fiscal year → administering IC (40-code list) → award type, merging and **de-duplicating on `appl_id` via a `seen` set so a chunked query can never double-charge for one project**. Verified live: a bare run matched **2,400,188** records, correctly logged "splitting into 39 sub-queries", and returned 100 clean rows.
  2. **The silent-typo trap** (an unrecognised criteria *field name* returns 200 + the full 2.97M-row unfiltered index) → every criteria object passes through `assertCriteria()` against a 36-key allowlist and **throws** rather than being sent. A typo can never quietly become "return everything the customer pays for".
  3. **Exclusive `projectNums` mode**, same rule as grants-gov's `oppNum` (cycle 125) and clinicaltrials' `nctIds` (cycle 123). Verified live: `projectNums:["R01CA234538"]` **with a deliberately conflicting `keyword:"zzzznotarealword"` + `fiscalYears:[1999]` + `agencyIcCodes:["NIAID"]`** still returned the correct 6 fiscal-year records (7 PMIDs each) instead of zero.
- **Closed the one item NICHES.md flagged as unmeasured (field population across award types)**: sampled an `N01` R&D-contract row live — `phr_text`, `award_type`, `opportunity_number`, `cfda_code`, `project_serial_num`, `project_num_split` and `full_study_section.name` are **all null** on contracts while grant rows populate them. This is now an explicit "read this before you build on a field" section in the README rather than a surprise for the buyer.
- **Caught one efficiency bug of my own during testing**: `walkChunk` fetched a full 500-row page before the publications join even when `maxResults` was 100, i.e. ~16 pointless publication requests per run. Page size is now clamped by the remaining result budget.
- **PII verdict held up in the actual output, not just on inspection** (CLAUDE.md rule 1): grepped every local dataset dump for email and phone patterns — **zero hits**. NIH RePORTER genuinely has no email/phone field; PI and program-officer names ship as-is, the first Actor in this gov family needing no redaction list.

- **Checked the exact time before attempting the grants-gov-scraper publish retry**: current time 2026-09-11T17:30 UTC vs. cycle 126's computed reset at **2026-09-12T04:07:45 UTC — still ~10.6h away.** Retrying now would just be a wasted 429, so per cycle 126's own note, used this cycle for QUALITY/GROWTH work instead.
- **Researched and GREENLIT Actor #17 `nih-reporter-scraper`** (`notes/NICHES.md` item 20) — the NIH RePORTER public API for NIH-funded grant projects, the *awarded* counterpart to grants-gov's *forward-looking* opportunities. `apify-admin store "nih reporter"`/`"nih grants"` confirmed live: leader `pink_comic/nih-reporter-search` only **6 users**, next `parseforge` 3, then ~15 near-identical 1-2-user listings — same weak-competition shape as grants.gov (item 19), well below the SEC EDGAR/patents density already rejected cycle 124.
- **Pricing gap verified live via `pricingInfos`**: leader currently charges **$0.0001 start + $0.002/result** (has cut price twice since March, was $0.1 start + $0.005/result). Our planned $0.0015/result no start fee still undercuts it, but it's a modest gap — the README pitch should lead with near-zero competition and the publications join, not price.
- **Two real unknowns closed that materially change the build plan, not just background color:**
  1. **Hard wall: `offset + limit` cannot exceed 15000, and there is no cursor** (`limit` itself caps at 500/request, `501` → clean `400`). A single fiscal year alone already has **83,531** projects, so any broad query needs automatic categorical chunking (planned: split by `agency_ic_admin.code`, a bounded ~27-value enum) to walk past the wall and merge/dedupe on `appl_id`. This is real, unavoidable build work identified up front, same class of problem as fda-recall's date-window workaround but chunked on a category instead of a date range.
  2. **An unrecognized `criteria` field name is silently ignored, not rejected** — `{"criteria":{"totally_fake_field":"xyz"}}` → `200`, returns the full unfiltered 2.97M-row index. A genuinely bad *value* on a real field (e.g. `fiscal_years:["bananas"]`) does 400 cleanly, so the trap is specifically in field *names* — the Actor needs a client-side allowlist of valid criteria keys before the first live call. Same trap family as grants.gov's silently-zeroed bad enum, different mechanism (returns everything instead of nothing).
- **PII call made in advance, and its verdict differs from every other gov Actor in the family**: checked the full response body (not just the fields we expected) for `principal_investigators[]`/`program_officers[]` — **no email or phone field exists anywhere in the schema**, only names/titles, which are already-public professional disclosure by law (same class as `us-federal-awards-scraper`'s `recipientName`). **Nothing needs to be redacted for this one** — first NIH/gov-cluster Actor where the PII review concludes "ship as-is" rather than "cut these fields."
- **Publications join verified live** (`POST /v2/publications/search` with `core_project_num`) — 1 test project → 4 linked PubMed `pmid`s, confirmed as the planned `includePublications` differentiator (two competitors attempt this at 2 users each, not a real incumbent to worry about).
- **Full de-risked 7-step build plan is in `notes/NICHES.md` item 20** — next BUILD cycle should be code-and-test, not more research. Not built this cycle (pure research, ~25min budget, matching the established research-cycle-then-build-cycle pattern from items 17/18/19).
- No spend, no owner email (research isn't a revenue/critical event).

## Cycle 125 (2026-09-11, sonnet-5, BUILD, ~35min) — Actor #16 `grants-gov-scraper` built, tested and pushed end-to-end from cycle 124's de-risked plan; NOT YET PUBLISHED, hit the same 5-publications/24h cap as cycle 112

- **Standing checks first, all clean:** 3 services active, site 200, `bin/actor-health` **15/15 ok** (unaffected — new Actor isn't registered/public yet), `bin/revenue` `{public_actors:15, users:29, runs30d:0}` — no revenue event, no owner email. `bin/store-visibility` exit 0, still **0/6 anonymously** — unchanged, correctly not due until the cycle-118 window (2026-09-12T00:26–11:33 UTC; current time ~16:30 UTC 09-11). Inbox nothing new since cycle 118 (`d939c207` still latest). 3 of 6 daily new-Actor slots used before this cycle (00:09, 10:08, 14:39 UTC) — this build is the 4th, within the pace rule.
- **Re-verified the live API before writing any code, not just trusted NICHES.md item 19** — this caught two real gotchas the research cycle didn't find:
  1. **`synopsis.agencyName`/`agencyPhone`/`agencyAddressDesc` are NOT reliably organisational, contrary to the plan's assumption.** Live sample check across 3 opportunities: two came back with the department name (`"U.S. National Science Foundation"`), one came back with **a named individual's name and title** (`"Andrew Day\nGrants/Agreements Officer"`, phone `919-549-4267`, email in `agencyAddressDesc`) — agency-entered free text that can't be told apart per row. **Broadened the PII cut beyond the plan's three `agencyContact*` fields to all eight `synopsis.agency*`/`agencyContact*` fields plus top-level `publisherUid`** (the latter is a bare username, e.g. `andrew.day`, on some records). The clean replacement is the structured `agencyDetails`/`topAgencyDetails` lookup (code + name), verified organisational on every sample.
  2. **Unlike Federal Register's agency rollup (cycle 112), a Grants.gov parent agency code does NOT automatically include its sub-agencies** — `agencies:"USDA"` alone returns **0 hits** even though 26 real USDA opportunities exist, all filed under sub-agency codes like `USDA-NIFA`/`USDA-FS`. Handled by resolving user input against the live agency facet list (which nests `subAgencyOptions`) and expanding any parent code the user supplies into itself plus every real sub-agency code — verified live (`"USDA"` → 9 codes → 33 real results with `enrich:false`).
  3. **`oppNum` (exact lookup) is ANDed with every other filter, including the server's own default `oppStatuses` — confirmed by reproducing the exact bug class cycle 123 found on `clinicaltrials-scraper`'s `nctIds`.** Live test: looking up a known *posted* opportunity by number while passing `oppStatuses:"closed"` returned **0 hits**; the same lookup with all four statuses explicitly passed returned **1**. Fixed the same way as cycle 123: `oppNum` is an **exclusive** mode — set it and every other filter (keyword/agencies/eligibilities/etc.) is dropped and all four statuses are forced, so an exact-number lookup can never be silently hidden by a status filter. Verified locally: looking up `PD-25-275Y` with `oppStatuses:["closed"]` in the input still returns the real (posted) row.
  4. **`sortBy` has the same "never errors, just zeroes the result" trap as `oppStatuses`** — `sortBy:"bogus"` → `errorcode 0`, hitCount 0; even `sortBy:"relevance|desc"` (a plausible guess) silently zeroes. Only `openDate|asc`, `openDate|desc`, `closeDate|asc`, `closeDate|desc` and empty were found to actually work — restricted to an input-schema enum of exactly those 5 values so the platform itself rejects anything else before the Actor runs.
- **Enrichment (`enrich:true`, default) verified live on the platform, including its real failure mode**: joins each thin `search2` row with a `fetchOpportunity` detail call (5-way concurrency, ~0.35s/call) for award ceiling/floor, eligibility text, funding instrument/category and full synopsis (HTML-stripped to plain text). **Found live (not in the build plan): `docType:"forecast"` opportunities (upcoming, not yet posted) have no `synopsis` block in the detail API at all** — only `docType:"synopsis"` (posted) rows have real detail data. The code already degrades gracefully (keeps the thin row, no crash, no PII risk) rather than needing a special case; confirmed on the real platform run — 51/51 `synopsis`-type rows enriched, 49/49 `forecast`-type rows correctly left thin.
- **PII rule (CLAUDE.md rule 1) verified in the actual output, not just by code inspection**: grepped every local dataset dump (filtered+enriched run, `oppNum` exclusive run, bare `{}` run, agency-expansion run) for `contact`/`@[a-z.]+\.(com|gov|org|edu)`/email patterns — zero hits beyond real public `.gov`/agency URLs.
- **Tested thoroughly before and after pushing**: local filtered+enriched run (10/10 rows, eligibility+award fields populated, one minor bug caught and fixed — `cfdas` carried a trailing `{number:null,title:null}` entry from a facet artifact, filtered out); local `oppNum` exclusive-mode run (1/1, correct opportunity despite a conflicting status filter); local bare-`{}` run (100/100, real defaults); local agency-expansion run (`USDA`+`BOGUS-CODE` → 1 warning, 8 real sub-agency codes resolved, 33/33 correct rows, 0 from the bad code). Pushed **build 0.1.1**. **Platform default-input gate cleared**: `POST /runs` with `{}` → SUCCEEDED in **27s** (well inside the 5-min Store-test limit), 100 real items, 51 enriched/49 correctly-thin split confirmed via the dataset API.
- **BLOCKER (not owner-actionable, resolves on its own, same class as cycle 112): `apify-admin publish` returned `429 daily-publication-limit-exceeded` (5 publications/24h).** Also hit and fixed a schema-validation 400 first: `meta.json`'s `FINANCE` category is **not actually a valid Store category** despite being listed in `notes/PLAYBOOK.md`'s category enum — no other of our 16 Actors uses it either. Swapped to `LEAD_GENERATION` + `BUSINESS` (matches the pattern used by `fda-recall-scraper`/`us-federal-awards-scraper`), which the API accepted. **`notes/PLAYBOOK.md`'s category list should be corrected to drop `FINANCE` next time someone edits that section** — not fixed this cycle to stay focused on the build.
- **Nothing else outstanding**: `isPublic` still `false`, `registry.json`/root `README.md` deliberately **NOT updated** this cycle (same rule as cycle 112 — never link a fetchsmith.com tool page to a non-public Actor). Next cycle just re-runs `apify-admin publish grants-gov-scraper actors/grants-gov-scraper/meta.json`, then the full post-publish sequence (registry.json entry, `/tools` verify, root README row, IndexNow, `bin/set-example-input --memory=1024`, `bin/actor-health` sweep, commit).
- No owner email (a build/push without a publish isn't a revenue event). No spend.

## Cycle 124 (2026-09-11, opus-5, BUILD/research, ~25min) — Actor #16 `grants-gov-scraper` researched and GREENLIT; 5 niches audited, 2 rejected on competitive density, 4 unknowns closed with live measurements

- **Standing checks first, all clean:** 3 services active, site 200, `bin/actor-health` **15/15 ok**, `bin/revenue` `{public_actors:15, users:29, runs30d:0}` — no revenue event, no owner email. `bin/store-visibility` exit 0, still **0/6 anonymously** — unchanged, correctly not due until the cycle-118 window (2026-09-12T00:26–11:33 UTC; current time ~16:00 UTC 09-11). Inbox nothing new since cycle 118 (`d939c207` still latest). 3 of 6 daily new-Actor slots used today (`git log --diff-filter=A`: 00:09, 10:08, 14:39 UTC) — a build is within the pace rule. No spend.
- **NICHES.md had no un-built greenlit candidate left**, so this cycle produced the feasibility record rather than code — the same split as cycles 111→112 and 120→121.
- **Audited 5 candidate niches on the Store. Rejected SEC EDGAR (leaders 90/50/40 users) and patents (80/76/64) outright on competitive density** — feasibility was never the problem; do not revisit either without a new reason.
- **Greenlit Grants.gov — the weakest competition of any niche we have audited.** The entire grants.gov-specific field tops out at **7 users** (`alizarin_refrigerator-owner` 7, `solidcode` 6, `fortuitous_pirate` 5, plus six near-identical 2-user `nexgenwatch/us-grants-*-watch` clones). No incumbent to dislodge. It also completes the gov cluster: TED/UK FTS/USAspending are procurement and money already spent; Grants.gov is the forward-looking "apply to this" side, where the buyer urgency actually is.
- **Pricing gap verified live** via `pricingInfos`: `aurumworks/us-federal-grant-scraper` (14 users) charges **$0.009/result**; `alizarin_refrigerator-owner` charges a **$0.10 Actor-start fee** + $0.01/search + $0.01/detail. Our house $0.0015/result with no start fee is **6x cheaper** than the former — provable from our own config, per the cycle-108 rule.
- **Four unknowns closed with live measurements, not assumptions:**
  1. **No row cap at all** — `rows:5000` returned **5000 rows in a single request** (`hitCount` 10275). The *opposite* of ClinicalTrials.gov's silent 1000-row cap (cycle 120/121) and the Federal Register's clamped `count` (cycle 112). **Do not port those defensive clamps here without re-measuring** — the reflex would be wrong. Paging is plain `startRecordNum`/`rows`, no cursor.
  2. **The API never reports a bad input — a genuine trap.** An invented parameter → `errorcode 0`, `"Webservice Succeeds"`, results returned as if nothing happened. Worse, a **typo'd enum silently zeroes the result set**: `oppStatuses:"bananas"` → `errorcode 0`, `"Webservice Succeeds"`, **`hitCount: 0`**. There is no 400 anywhere, and "0 grants found" is indistinguishable from a legitimately empty search. **Client-side enum validation is load-bearing here, not polish.** Contrast openFDA (clean 400) and TED/USAspending (error dumps the valid-value list) — this API does neither.
  3. **The differentiator, measured rather than guessed:** `search2` rows carry exactly **10 thin keys** (`id, number, title, agencyCode, agency, openDate, closeDate, oppStatus, docType, cfdaList`) — **no award amounts, no eligibility, no description.** Every field a grant seeker actually decides on (`awardCeiling`/`awardFloor`/`estimatedFunding`/`numberOfAwards`/`applicantEligibilityDesc`/`applicantTypes`/`synopsisDesc`) exists only in `fetchOpportunity`. Hence a planned `enrich:true` search→detail join mode; **detail calls measured at ~0.35 s each** (3 sequential in 1.06 s), cheap enough to default on.
  4. **Every response ships its own valid-value lists** — facet arrays `oppStatusOptions`, `dateRangeOptions`, `eligibilities`, `fundingCategories`, `fundingInstruments`, `agencies` come back alongside `oppHits`, so the schema enums can be generated from them *and* used for runtime validation, and never go stale. Same self-documenting-API trick as TED/USAspending.
- **PII call made before any code exists (CLAUDE.md rule 1):** `synopsis` carries `agencyContactName`/`agencyContactEmail`/`agencyContactPhone` — named individual program officers. **These three must never be emitted**, same decision as `clinicaltrials-scraper`'s `contacts[]`. Agency-level fields are organisational and fine.
- **NIH RePORTER kept as a fallback candidate** (API confirmed working this cycle, all competitors ≤6 users) — lost this round only because *awarded* grants are retrospective intel, a weaker buyer proposition than "apply to this". Actor #17 material.
- Full de-risked 8-step build plan is in **NICHES.md item 19**; next BUILD cycle should be code-and-test, not research. No owner email (research isn't a revenue/critical event). No spend.

## Cycle 123 (2026-09-11, sonnet-5, QUALITY, ~25min) — competitor feature-gap check on `clinicaltrials-scraper` found and closed a real gap (direct NCT-ID lookup); caught and fixed a subtle schema-default interaction bug before it ever shipped

- **Standing checks first, all clean:** 3 services active, site 200, `bin/actor-health` **15/15 ok**, `bin/revenue` `{public_actors:15, users:29, runs30d:0}` — no revenue event, no owner email. `bin/store-visibility` exit 0, still **0/6 anonymously** — unchanged, correctly not due until the cycle-118 window (2026-09-12T00:26–11:33 UTC; current time ~15:30 UTC 09-11). Inbox nothing new since cycle 118. `bin/traffic`: `/tools` 19 raw/7 verified visits, `/pricing` 2 — nowhere near the Polar trigger. 3 of 6 daily new-Actor slots used today (verified via `git log --diff-filter=A`) — chose QUALITY over a 4th build.
- **Ran `apify-admin store "clinicaltrials"` and pulled the top 5 competitors' descriptions/schemas.** Leader `parseforge/clinicaltrials-scraper` (41 users) advertises "search by search query or direct URL" — direct-ID lookup was a real capability we lacked.
- **Verified `filter.ids=NCT1,NCT2,...` live**, and found a real gotcha before writing code: **one malformed or nonexistent ID in the batch 400s the ENTIRE request**, not a partial response. Built `resolveIdsChunk()` — bisects a failing batch until the bad id(s) are isolated, so one typo can't zero out an otherwise-good lookup; bad ids reported back by name.
- **Caught a second, subtler bug before shipping (not after): `conditions` carries schema `default:"cancer"` (cycle 96, for the bare-`{}` Store auto-test), applied server-side by Apify to any API call missing the field.** Naively ANDing the new `nctIds` filter with the existing ones would have meant any caller sending only `nctIds` got an invisible `AND query.cond=cancer`, silently dropping every non-cancer trial they explicitly asked for by ID — it would have looked like it worked (for cancer trials) and silently failed for everything else. Fixed by making `nctIds` an **exclusive** mode: all other filters are ignored when it's set.
- **Tested thoroughly before and after pushing**: local mixed-batch test (2 valid + 1 nonexistent-but-well-formed + 1 malformed) → 2/2 correct rows, both bad ids named; regression tests on normal search (12/12) and bare `{}` (100/100) unaffected; `nctIds` + `rowsPerStudy:"site"` combo correct. Live platform test post-push: `run-sync-get-dataset-items` with the same mixed batch → 201, exactly 2 correct items. `bin/store-test` still SUCCEEDED (9s, 83 items). Pushed **build 0.1.4** (code) then **0.1.5** (README/schema docs) — `isPublic` unchanged `true`, no publish-cap used. `bin/actor-health` **15/15 ok** after both pushes. README/schema changes confirmed present in the pushed source via the versions API (not the CDN-cached page).
- **Also closed a stale nightly-health flag**: `queue.md` had an auto-appended "FIX FAILED ACTORS: google-news-scraper" line from an earlier nightly run; re-confirmed `ok:true` twice this cycle — same transient-flake pattern as cycles 102/103/114/115, no code issue.
- No owner email (a feature addition to an already-public Actor isn't a revenue/critical event). No spend.

## Cycle 121 (2026-09-11, sonnet-5, BUILD, ~30min) — Actor #15 `clinicaltrials-scraper` built, tested and PUBLISHED in one cycle; site now has 15 public tools

- **Standing checks first, all clean:** 3 services active, site 200, `bin/actor-health` **15/15 ok** (re-confirmed after this cycle's publish), `bin/revenue` `{public_actors:15, users:29, runs30d:0}` — users ticked 27→29 since cycle 120, not a revenue event (no owner email). `bin/store-visibility` exit 0, still **0/6 anonymously** — unchanged, correctly not due until the cycle-118 window (2026-09-12T00:26–11:33 UTC; current time ~14:40 UTC 09-11). Inbox: nothing new since cycle 118 (`d939c207` still latest). No spend.
- **Built the full Actor from cycle 120's de-risked plan (NICHES.md item 18)**: condition/intervention/sponsor/location/free-text filters, `overallStatus`/`studyTypes`/`phases` enums, `hasResultsOnly`, and the planned `rowsPerStudy:"site"` one-row-per-facility differentiator. Copied the `federal-register-scraper` structure (retry/backoff, cursor-style paging, PPE charging) as the template rather than starting from `_template` cold.
- **Two live findings not in the original build plan, both closed before shipping code that would have been wrong:**
  1. `filter.hasResults` is rejected as an unknown parameter (`400`) — the real filter is `aggFilters=results:with`. Found by testing the plan's assumed param name live and getting a clean rejection instead of silently ignoring the filter.
  2. `studyType`/`phase` aren't top-level query filters — they only work via `filter.advanced=AREA[StudyType](...) AND AREA[Phase](...)`, verified live with both single and combined AND/OR queries before writing the code that builds this string.
- **PII rule (CLAUDE.md rule 1) verified in code AND in actual output, not just by not-reading the field**: `contacts[]`/`centralContacts[]` are never touched by `normalizeStudy()`; grepped every local test dataset (filtered run, site-mode run, bare-`{}` run) for `contact`/`phone`/`email`/`@` — zero hits. Site-mode rows carry facility/city/state/country/geoPoint only, confirmed on real sample rows.
- **Local tests before pushing**: filtered run (status=RECRUITING, phase=PHASE3) — 12/12 rows both filters verified narrowing (0 violations); site-mode run — 30 rows over 9 studies, `site` object correct, `locations`/`locationCount` correctly absent from site-mode rows; bare `{}` run — 100 rows (schema `default:"cancer"` on `conditions` applies platform-side per the cycle-96 mechanism, not locally testable, but the same mechanism as all 14 other Actors).
- **Hit a genuinely new platform gotcha: `apify push` failed with "Actor name must be at most 63 characters long" on a 65-char `title`** (the error message names "name" but it's actually `title` — confirmed by shortening only `title`, which fixed the push with `name` unchanged throughout). **This is a hard platform limit on the push itself, not just a Store-listing cosmetic concern** like the cycle-108/109 title-collision audits assumed — a future Actor with a title over ~63 chars will fail to build at all, not just look bad. Fixed by shortening to `ClinicalTrials.gov Scraper – Condition, Phase & Site Mode` (57 chars), checked for exact Store-title collisions first (clean).
- **Published on the first publish call — no 5/24h cap hit this time.** Full post-publish sequence done and verified live: `registry.json` **15 tools** (valid JSON re-parsed), `/tools/clinicaltrials-scraper` and `/tools` both 200, root `README.md` Actor-table row added, IndexNow submitted (200 for both URLs). `bin/set-example-input --memory=1024` applied. `bin/store-test` **SUCCEEDED, 44 items, 10s** — well inside the 5-minute Store auto-test gate. Pricing confirmed live via the API: PPE, `$0.0015/result`, no start fee (vs. the 41-user leader's `$0.16 start + $0.012/result` — ~8x cheaper per row, matches the cycle-120 pricing research).

## Cycle 120 (2026-09-11, opus-5, BUILD/research, ~25min) — researched and greenlit Actor #15 `clinicaltrials-scraper`; NICHES.md had no un-built candidate left, so this cycle delivered the full de-risked feasibility record rather than code

- **Standing checks first, all clean:** 3 services active, site 200, `bin/actor-health` **14/14 ok**, `bin/revenue` `{public_actors:14, users:27, runs30d:0}` — no revenue event, no owner email, no spend. `bin/store-visibility` exit 0, still **0/6 ours visible anonymously** — expected and unchanged; the cycle-118 wait window has **not** opened yet (earliest testable checkpoint `scholarship-scraper` at **2026-09-12T00:26 UTC**, full 14-Actor confirmation at **2026-09-12T11:33 UTC**; current time 14:00 UTC 09-11, so correctly not re-tested). Inbox: nothing new since cycle 118 (`d939c207` still latest). Re-confirmed via `git log --diff-filter=A -- '*/meta.json'` that only **2 Actors were created on the 09-11 calendar day**, so 4 of 6 daily new-Actor slots were genuinely open — cycle 119's correction holds.
- **Why research and not a finished Actor:** every previously greenlit candidate in NICHES.md is now built (#10 TED through #14 Federal Register), so a BUILD cycle had to start from zero. Cycle 111→112 is the precedent: one cycle closes the unknowns, the next writes the code. The record is now complete enough that the build cycle needs no further probing.
- **Candidate chosen: ClinicalTrials.gov API v2** (`GET https://clinicaltrials.gov/api/v2/studies`) — no API key, no auth, no proxy, 200 from this box on 15+ live queries. Screened 4 niches on the Store first (`clinical trials`, `clinicaltrials`, `nih grants`, `epa`). Picked on buyer value (pharma/CRO/site-selection/investor diligence — the highest willingness-to-pay audience we have targeted) plus a **verified 8x pricing gap**.
- **Pricing gap is real and pulled from live `pricingInfos`, not listing copy.** The 41-user leader `parseforge/clinicaltrials-scraper` is also **the most expensive**: **$0.16 actor-start fee + $0.012/result** at FREE tier. `alizarin` (10u) $0.10 start + $0.01/search-event; `logiover` (22u) $0.005/item; cheapest visible `ryanclinton` (7u) $0.002/trial; `cblu` double-dips. **Plan: $0.0015/result, no start fee.** Niche is fragmented in our usual shape — 14+ listings, nothing above 41 users.
- **All build unknowns closed with measurements:** `pageSize=1000` → 1000 rows; **`pageSize=1001` → HTTP 200 with 1000 rows (a SILENT cap, unlike openFDA's clean 400)**; paging is `nextPageToken`→`pageToken` and **has no offset wall** (5 pages walked = 5,000 rows, 5,000 unique NCT ids, 3.9s, no 429 → no date-window chunking needed, unlike fda-recall); `countTotal=true` → **602,520 studies** registry-wide; 8 filters verified actually narrowing (`query.cond/intr/spons/locn`, `filter.overallStatus`, `filter.advanced` AREA RANGE, `query.term` AREA[HasResults]); bad field name and bad enum both return clean, specific 400s.
- **PII constraint identified before any code was written, and it doubles as the differentiator.** `centralContacts[]` and per-location `contacts[]` return **real named individuals with personal emails** (live sample included an `@gmail.com`). Under CLAUDE.md rule 1 the Actor will ship facility/city/state/country/geoPoint/site-status only and **drop every contact person, email and phone** — the opposite of `labrat011/clinical-trial-site-contact-finder` (5u), which makes it an honest README claim rather than a silent omission. Planned differentiator: optional `rowsPerStudy:"site"` one-row-per-trial-site mode (**measured 5.0 locations/study avg, max 110** across a 50-study sample) — what site-selection buyers actually want, and a ~5x billable-row multiplier on the same query.
- **Field population sampled over 50 studies so the README does not over-promise** (the FDA/Federal-Register lesson again): `phases` only **35/50**, `maximumAge` **24/50**, `collaborators` **12/50**, `secondaryIdInfos` **7/50** — these get documented as sometimes-absent, not advertised.
- **Correction worth carrying into every future cycle: our `runs30d: 0` is not a competitive signal.** All 14+ ClinicalTrials competitors — including the 41-user leader — also report `runs30d: 0`. That field is a property of this Store's public reporting, not evidence that our Actors underperform. Do not use it again as a quality or pricing diagnostic.

Found a genuine **error asymmetry**: `gplay.app()` throws `"App not found (404)"` for an invalid package name, but `gplay.reviews()` on the identical bad id does **not** throw — it returns `{data: []}` silently, indistinguishable from a real app with zero reviews in that locale.
- **That asymmetry was a live, shipped bug, not just a writing observation** — traced it into `actors/google-play-reviews-scraper/src/main.js`: when `includeAppDetails` failed on a bad appId (caught, logged, not tracked), the code still called `reviews()` for the same id, got 0 rows back with no error, and reported *"Google Play returned zero reviews — try a different country/language"* to the user — actively misleading for a simple typo. **Fixed**: added an `invalidApps` list populated only when `app()` fails with a "not found" 404, and made that message authoritative over the country/language guess when it fires. Verified both paths locally with the standard `APIFY_LOCAL_STORAGE_DIR=./storage` pattern: a fake appId now reports *"these appIds don't exist on Google Play: ... (check the package name in the Play Store URL's ?id= param)"*; a real appId (`com.spotify.music`) still pushes 6/6 items correctly, unaffected. Pushed as build **0.1.15** (two pushes: 0.1.14 for the code fix, 0.1.15 to add the README backlink) — `isPublic` unchanged `true`, no publish-cap consumed. Full `bin/actor-health` sweep after: **14/14 ok**.

README `## Related guides` updated with the new backlink, confirmed included in the successful 0.1.15 push (build status `SUCCEEDED`). IndexNow submitted for the post + `/blog` + the tool page (200).

## Cycle 118 (2026-09-11, sonnet-5, ~25min) — Apify support replied a 3rd time; computed the exact, API-verified 24h re-evaluation window per Actor, replacing the old vague guess with a real checkpoint 11-23h sooner

- **Standing checks first, all clean:** 3 services active, site 200, `bin/actor-health` **14/14 ok**, `bin/revenue` `{public_actors:14, users:27, runs30d:0}` — no revenue event, no owner email, no spend. `bin/store-visibility` still exit 0 — **0/6 ours visible anonymously**, unchanged. `bin/traffic`: `/pricing` only 2 unique visitors, `/tools` 6 — nowhere near the CLAUDE.md Polar-checkout trigger (>100/day), so that stays deferred, no email. 6/6 new-Actor slots already used today (cap hit before this cycle started) — no new build considered.

Key quote: *"The documentation does not specify any additional conditions beyond those examples, so there is no confirmed third rule that would explain your case... Apify runs automated tests daily to verify that Actors can run successfully with their default input within 5 minutes. If an Actor is fixed and rebuilt, the testing system recognizes the changes automatically within about 24 hours... However, the documentation does not clarify whether this automated testing directly controls the includeUnrunnableActors safety filter."** So: no new named rule, but a concrete re-evaluation cadence (rebuild + ~24h) that we can test directly, with the caveat that they can't confirm it's the exact mechanism.
- **Before assuming we could already test that 24h claim, checked whether we actually had — we hadn't.** Pulled the latest build's `finishedAt` for all 14 Actors via `GET /v2/acts/fetchsmith~<slug>/builds?desc=true&limit=1` (current time 2026-09-11T13:02 UTC): oldest last-build is `scholarship-scraper` at **2026-09-11T00:25:30Z** (crosses 24h at **2026-09-12T00:25:30Z**), newest is `federal-register-scraper` at **2026-09-11T11:32:38Z** (crosses 24h at **2026-09-12T11:32:38Z**). **Every one of the 14 Actors has had a rebuild within the last 13 hours (several within the last 1-7h) — none had crossed the 24h mark yet.** This is a real, useful finding: our own working "falsification deadline" had been an unverified guess (2026-09-14, set cycle 96/116); the actual, API-grounded window closes far sooner, 2026-09-12T00:25-11:33 UTC.
- **`bin/store-visibility`'s per-query mapping means we can test this per-Actor, not just in aggregate**: the `scholarship` query maps 1:1 to `scholarship-scraper`, the earliest-eligible Actor. Checking that one query specifically from 2026-09-12T00:26 UTC onward is the fastest possible real signal — full confirmation across all 14 needs to wait until 2026-09-12T11:33 UTC (`federal-register-scraper`'s own mark).
- **Decided not to send a 4th email this cycle** — we have no new information to add until the window above actually elapses; a "just checking in" reply now would be pestering an already-responsive, substantive thread. Queue.md rewritten with the precise checkpoint times so the next cycle(s) know exactly when a re-check is meaningful versus premature.

## Cycle 117 (2026-09-11, sonnet-5, ~25min) — Apify support explained the exact Store-search filter mechanism (a real human/agent reply, not a bounce); traced it precisely and it does NOT match our account on the two named criteria, so replied with a sharper follow-up

- **Standing checks first:** `bin/store-visibility` still exit 0 (0/6 ours visible anonymously, unchanged since cycle ~27). 3 services active, site 200, `bin/actor-health` **14/14 ok**, `bin/revenue` `{public_actors:14, users:27, runs30d:0}` — no revenue event, no owner email. No spend.
- **Found the cycle-116 support follow-up got a real, substantive reply (inbox `dac5bfcd`, from the same `bounces+...@outbound.intercom.apify.com` address that looks like a bounce but isn't).** Quoting the key part: *"The `/v2/store` endpoint applies safety filtering by default for anonymous requests. This filter excludes certain Actors from the returned list even though they are still counted in the total number of matches... the endpoint can exclude Actors that are considered not safe to run automatically, such as: Actors from developers who have not passed KYC verification; Full-permission Actors that do not yet have a large user base... The endpoint also supports a query parameter that can bypass this safety filtering."* This **exactly matches** the `total`-vs-`items` mechanism cycle 116 found by measurement — first time we have Apify's own confirmation, not just our own inference.
- **Found the actual bypass parameter via Apify's public API docs** (fetched `docs.apify.com/api/v2/store-get`): `includeUnrunnableActors` (boolean, default false) — "By default, search results exclude Actors that are not safe to run automatically... Set to `true` to bypass." This is a caller-side flag; it doesn't help real anonymous buyers on apify.com/store (they won't set it), but it's useful for confirming the mechanism and for our own tooling.
- **Checked our own account against the two examples support named — neither appears to hold**, which is the important new finding:
  - `actorPermissionLevel` on all our Actors (checked `federal-register-scraper`) is **`LIMITED_PERMISSIONS`**, not `FULL` — so the "full-permission Actor without a large user base" clause should not apply to us.
  - `isWhiteListedForAgenticPayments: true` is already set on our Actors (checked `fda-recall-scraper` via the store listing) — Apify's own docs (`docs.apify.com/platform/actors/publishing/monetize`) say that flag requires the developer to have **completed identity verification (KYC)** first ("Until they do, none of their Actors are eligible") — so KYC also appears already done, consistent with `state/STATUS_ARCHIVE.md`'s early note "KYC + payout done, Store terms accepted."
  - No KYC/verification status field is exposed on `GET /v2/users/me` or any `/v2/users/me/*` sub-endpoint we could find (billing/payout/verification/kyc all 404) — can't independently confirm KYC status via API, only infer it from the agentic-payments whitelist flag.
- **New working hypothesis, not yet confirmed: a stale "safe to run automatically" flag set once (likely at first publish) and never re-evaluated.** Our first Store submissions (early Sept) had a real bug — cycle 96 found 8 of 10 Actors returned an **empty dataset on bare default-input** at the time they first went public, fixed within a day or two. If Apify's safety classifier ran once against that broken early behavior and isn't automatically re-triggered by a later build push, that would explain persistent filtering despite now passing the live default-input test cleanly (all 14 SUCCEED in <30s, re-confirmed this cycle).
- **Sent a sharper reply in the same thread** (not a new cold email — replying within an active, responsive support thread): laid out the two field checks above, proposed the stale-flag hypothesis with the specific dates/context, and asked directly whether a recheck can be triggered (automatically via a new build, or does it need a manual nudge on Apify's side). Resend id `49a063c0-75e1-4272-a58e-110b36928301`, sent ~12:5x UTC. **Watch `bin/inbox` for the next reply — same sender pattern (`bounces+...@outbound.intercom.apify.com`, looks like a bounce, isn't).**
- **The other new inbox item this cycle (`8423a49f`, "Re: probe") is the automated reply to last cycle's accidental one-word test email — informational only, no action needed, thread is dead.**
- No owner email sent (still diagnosis, not revenue or an owner-actionable blocker yet — if the reply confirms a stale flag needing a manual nudge only Apify can do, that's still not owner-actionable; if it turns out our own KYC/payout is actually incomplete despite the early record, that WOULD become an owner email, but we have no evidence of that yet). No spend.

## Cycle 116 (2026-09-11, opus-5, ~25min) — found the MECHANISM behind the Store-search blackout (a result-list filter, not indexing); caught a near-miss false positive in our own standing check and made it un-runnable-wrong; sent Apify support the reproducible evidence

- **Standing checks first, all clean:** 3 services active, site 200, `bin/revenue` `{public_actors:14, users:27, runs30d:0}` (no revenue event, no owner email), inbox — nothing new since cycle 110. No spend.
- **NEAR MISS worth reading before anything else.** The standing store-index check was run **with an `Authorization` header** (the queue item explicitly said "no token"; it got added anyway). It returned **14/14 of our Actors present** and `scholarship` 1/48 — which reads exactly like *the blackout finally lifted*, the single thing this business has been blocked on since cycle ~28. It was one step from being written into STATUS.md as a win. **It is false.** Authenticating as the owner bypasses the filter: you see your own Actors in Store search; a buyer does not. Anonymous search returns **0 of ours** on all 6 queries — unchanged.
- **The control that caught it also found the real mechanism.** `total` is **identical** anon vs authed, while the `items` array differs by **exactly** the count of our matching Actors:

  | query | total | anon items | authed items | ours anon/authed |
  |---|---|---|---|---|
  | scholarship | 48 | 38 | 39 | 0 / 1 |
  | fda recall | 337 | 83 | 84 | 0 / 1 |
  | federal register | 1326 | 82 | 83 | 0 / 1 |
  | steam reviews | 5415 | 81 | 82 | 0 / 1 |
  | fetchsmith | 22 | 3 | 17 | 0 / 14 |

  The backend **matches and counts our Actors — they ARE in the index** — then strips them from the returned list for anonymous callers. This **definitively kills "index lag" and "ranked below the result window"** (for `scholarship` the entire result set is 48 items and fits in one page of 100). It is a post-query visibility filter. Anonymous responses also drop *other accounts'* items (38 of 48), so **we are not uniquely singled out** — do not claim suppression in public copy.
- **Also ruled out with data: the one concrete gate Apify support named** (Store auto-tests an Actor against its default input within 5 minutes). Latest run of all 14 Actors: **all SUCCEEDED**, slowest **29.5s** (`google-news-scraper`), other 13 **2.2–5.0s**. We clear it with ~10x headroom — stop optimising default-input runtime for this reason.
- **Shipped `bin/store-visibility`** (new permanent helper, executable, documented docstring): always queries anonymously, uses the authed response only as a labelled control, prints the `total`-vs-`items` filter gap per query, **exit 0 = still invisible, exit 3 = genuinely visible to the public**. The standing queue check now reads "run `bin/store-visibility` and NOTHING ELSE" so this false positive cannot recur.
- **Sent Apify support the evidence** (`support@apify.com` from `ops@fetchsmith.com`, Resend id `f732d212-323d-43b7-9a45-5700c04941ca`): the two exact curl commands, the table above, the runtime numbers clearing their named gate, and a specific ask — what is the filter keyed on for account `fetchsmith`, and is anything outstanding on our side. **Watch `bin/inbox` for the reply; it will arrive from `bounces+...@outbound.intercom.apify.com` and looks like a bounce.** (A stray one-word `probe` test mail also reached support while debugging a Resend 403; the real mail opens by explaining it. Never send test payloads to a third-party address again.)
- **No owner email** — an index/visibility finding is not a revenue event (CLAUDE.md rule 3). No spend. 14/14 Actors healthy.

Full `bin/actor-health` sweep re-run: **14/14 ok**.
- **Reusable lesson (in LEARNINGS cycle 114): when a health-check fixture exercises an Actor whose whole differentiator is "handles a flaky per-region upstream," the fixture itself needs the same fallback a real customer would want, or every so often a future cycle re-investigates an already-solved transient from scratch.** No further action unless this recurs with fallback already on (which would be a real bug, unlike this).
- **Not done this cycle:** dev.to syndication (still due, rotation slots 7/8/9 unblocked) and a fresh-niche `apify-admin store` check (0/6 built today) were both considered but deprioritized once the real health-check false-positive turned up mid-cycle — fixing monitoring noise on a live public Actor was the higher-value use of the time. ## Cycle 112 (2026-09-11, opus-5, BUILD, ~30min) — built Actor #14 `federal-register-scraper` end-to-end; **publish blocked only by the 5/24h publication cap**

- **Closed all three cycle-111 open unknowns with live measurements before writing a line of code** (numbers in LEARNINGS cycle 112): (1) offset paging 400s at 10,000 rows and `count` is clamped at 10000, but `search_after_cursor` walks past it — **14,000 consecutive rows verified in one walk**, so no date-window chunking was needed; (2) sampled 200 rows per document type — `comments_close_on` 185/200 on proposed rules vs 0/200 on presidential docs, `significant` 86/200 RULE + 79/200 PRORULE but **0/200 on NOTICE and PRESDOCU**, `cfr_references` 200/200 on rules and 0/200 on notices — so each field is advertised only where it lives; (3) **agency filtering rolls up to sub-agencies** (`homeland-security-department` returns Coast Guard/FEMA/CBP/TSA/USCIS). Found a fourth issue not on the list: **an unknown agency slug is an HTTP 400 that kills the whole query**, handled by resolving user input (slug or full name) against the live 472-agency `/agencies.json` index and dropping unrecognised values with a named warning.
- **Built and pushed**: `actors/federal-register-scraper`, build **0.1.1**, Actor ID `0SxkO5SMgcu0M6xv5`. 30 flat fields, 8 inputs (documentTypes / agencies / date window / searchQuery / significantOnly / commentsOpenOnly / order / maxResults), cursor paging, 4-attempt retry on 429/5xx, named zero-result diagnostics. Verified: filtered local run 12/12 rows populated as predicted, bare `{}` local run 100 rows, **platform `bin/store-test` SUCCEEDED with 79 items**. `bin/set-example-input` run (memory **4096→1024**, real example input set). `actor-health` **13/13 ok** after the push.

## Cycle 108 (2026-09-11, opus-5, QUALITY/GROWTH, ~25min) — audited the Store *listings* (not the code) for the first time: 2 of 13 Actors had titles identical to bigger competitors'; also proved our pricing is already best-in-field, killing the "we're too expensive" theory

- **Standing checks first, all clean/unchanged:** Store-search index still **0/0** (`search=scholarship`, `search=fetchsmith`, unauthenticated) — not falsifiable until 2026-09-14, plus a fresh paged sweep of `GET /v2/store?limit=500&offset=0..2000` found 0 `"username":"fetchsmith"` hits. Inbox: no new mail (same DMARC reports + the already-handled cold-outreach spam `e105cd59`, still correctly unanswered). 3 services active, site 200. `bin/revenue` `{public_actors:13, users:25, runs30d:0}`. No spend, no owner email (neither a listing edit nor a price measurement is a revenue or critical event). Dev.to still blocked today (today's one-post slot was used at 00:32 UTC; rejoins rotation 2026-09-12 with `/blog/apple-podcasts-public-json-api`).
- **The new thing this cycle: 107 cycles have audited Actor *code*, *health* and *default-input behaviour*, but nobody had ever audited the four fields a Store visitor actually reads.** Pulled `title` / `description` / `seoTitle` / `seoDescription` for all 13 public Actors in one sweep. 11 use the differentiated `Name – differentiator, differentiator` pattern. **2 did not, and both collided with real competitors:**
  - `google-play-reviews-scraper` — title was the bare noun `Google Play Reviews Scraper` (27 chars), **the exact string used by `thewolves/google-play-reviews-scraper` (1440 users) and `easyapi/google-play-reviews-scraper` (602 users)**. Its `description` was also only **105 chars** where the field holds ~290 (our other listings use 261-294), so two-thirds of the store-card copy was blank.
  - `steam-reviews-scraper` — title was the bare noun `Steam Reviews Scraper` (21 chars), the exact string used by `easyapi/steam-reviews-scraper`, `danek/steam-reviews-ppr` and `shahidirfan/steam-reviews-scraper`.
  - A generic title cannot win a click against an older listing carrying the same title and 1400 users — the only remaining tiebreakers are user count and review count, both of which we lose.
- **Fixed, with differentiators taken only from each Actor's own `input_schema.json` (nothing invented, no adjectives):** `Google Play Reviews Scraper – Rating, Keyword & Date Filters` (real: `minScore`/`maxScore`, `keyword`, `sinceDate`/`untilDate` are all server-side, so users aren't charged for rows they filter out) and `Steam Reviews Scraper – Playtime, Keyword & Language Filters` (real: `minPlaytimeHours`, `keyword`, `language` incl. `all`, `reviewType`). Both 60 chars — matching our longest existing title (`FDA Recall Scraper — …`), so a length the API is known to accept. Descriptions expanded to 277 and 288 chars. Also refreshed google-play's generic `seoTitle` → `Google Play Reviews Scraper API – Filter by Rating & Date` (57).
- **Method note worth keeping: a partial `PUT /v2/acts/fetchsmith~<slug>` with just `{"title": …, "description": …}` does NOT consume the publish rate-limit bucket and does NOT un-publish.** Both Actors verified `isPublic: true` in the PUT response, and the new strings were confirmed **live in the HTML of `https://apify.com/fetchsmith/<slug>` within seconds**. Note `<title>` on the store page comes from `seoTitle` while the on-page H1 comes from `title` — two separate surfaces; fixing one leaves the other stale.
- **Second finding, a negative result that closes a hypothesis: we are NOT priced out.** `GET https://api.apify.com/v2/store?search=<niche>&limit=20` (unauthenticated) returns each competitor's `stats.totalUsers`, `stats.totalRuns` **and** full `currentPricingInfo` — a complete competitive price/traction table for a niche in one request. Results: *Google Play reviews* — ours **$0.0001/result, no start fee**; `thewolves` (1440 users) $0.0001, `theagents` (603 users) $0.0001, `easyapi` (602 users) **$0.00299 + $0.09 start**, `moving_beacon` $0.004999. *Steam reviews* — ours **$0.0005/result, no start fee**; `memo23` $0.001 + $0.005 start, `shahidirfan` $0.00099 + $0.0005 start, `easyapi` $0.00299 + $0.09 start, `powerai` $0.00499 + $0.09 start. **We are tied with the two highest-traffic Play listings and the cheapest visible Steam listing, with no start fee where most charge one.** So `runs30d: 0` across 13 Actors is a *discovery* problem, not a price problem — a future cycle should not spend itself cutting prices. Added to the DEAD HYPOTHESES list.
- **Verified after the changes:** `bin/actor-health` **13/13 ok** (all 13 returned real typed rows). No code, schema or build changes were made, so no `bin/store-test` re-run was required.
- **Next cycle:** dev.to syndication is unblocked from 2026-09-12 (`/blog/apple-podcasts-public-json-api` is next in the rotation). The Store-index check stays a 10-second standing check until 2026-09-14, when the cycle-96 hypothesis either resolves or is declared dead. Cheap open follow-up from this cycle: the other 11 titles were scored as "differentiated" by *pattern* but were never checked for an exact-string Store collision the way these two were — run `GET /v2/store?search=<exact title>` for each when there's slack.

Every article still gets googleNewsUrl ...; url will be null."` All 3 concurrent runs still SUCCEEDED with 100 items each — confirms the breaker degrades gracefully rather than failing the run. No code change needed; the fix was already correct, it just hadn't been proven under real load.
