## Cycle 104 (2026-09-11, opus-5, DIAGNOSTIC/fix, ~30min) — reproduced Apify's automated Store test for the first time (empty default input, 5-min limit) and it caught TWO real production bugs that 100 cycles of `test_input.json`-based health checks could never see

- **The method (new, reusable): `bin/actor-health` has always run each Actor with its hand-written `test_input.json`. Apify support's only concrete statement (cycle 93 reply, inbox `455ec0c7`) was that Store Actors "are subject to automated testing to verify they can run successfully with their default input within a 5-minute limit". Nobody had ever run that test.** This cycle launched all 12 public Actors with **empty input `{}`** (so the platform applies `input_schema.json` defaults — exactly what Apify's test does) at each Actor's own default memory, and measured status + duration + item count.
- **Result: 10/12 pass cleanly (18-78s, 50-294 items). 2/12 FAIL the store-test shape:**
  - **`google-news-scraper`: still RUNNING at 250s with 15 items** — would have taken ~30 min. Log showed Google 429ing the URL-decode endpoint on *every single article*, with the code backing off up to 15s *per article* and continuing for all 100. So the default run (a) blows the 5-minute limit, (b) burns ~30 min of the user's paid compute, (c) returns `url: null` for 100% of rows. This is the cycle-12 prediction ("if a user reports url:null en masse it's Google's per-IP 429") actually happening on the platform IP.
  - **`eu-ted-tenders-scraper`: SUCCEEDED in 3s with 0 items.** Log: `TED API returned 429 on page 1` at 277ms, then `Done. Pushed 0 notices.` — exit code 0. **This is the real bug cycles 102 and 103 were chasing and missed.** Both cycles assumed the empty-dataset failures were the "transient empty page" case and hardened the *rows-empty* retry (0.1.6, then 0.1.7 with 3 retries/backoff). But a **429 was never retried at all**: `gotScraping` runs with `throwHttpErrors:false`, so got's own `retry:{limit:3}` never fires on a non-2xx — the code warned, `break`ed, and exited SUCCEEDED-with-0-items.
- **Fixes shipped and verified:**
  - `eu-ted-tenders-scraper` **build 0.1.8**: split `fetchPage` into `fetchPage`/`fetchPageOnce` with an explicit transient-status retry (408/425/429/500/502/503/504, delays 2s/5s/10s/20s, honours `Retry-After` capped at 30s). Plus: **a run that pushed 0 items because of an HTTP error now `Actor.fail()`s with a plain-English message** instead of exiting 0 — a silent empty dataset reads to a customer as "no tenders match", which is a lie. Genuinely-empty-but-healthy runs now set a status message suggesting which filter to widen.
  - `google-news-scraper` **build 0.1.15**: circuit breaker on the decode endpoint — after **4 consecutive** 429s, decoding is switched off for the rest of the run (one clear warning, `googleNewsUrl` still populated on every row), and a success resets the consecutive counter so scattered 429s don't trip it. The end-of-run status message now says decoding was switched off.
  - **Verified by re-running the exact same empty-input test: TED 0 items/3s -> `SUCCEEDED` 100 items/11s; google-news >250s-and-still-going/15 items -> `SUCCEEDED` 100 items/91s.** Full `bin/actor-health` sweep after both pushes: **12/12 ok**.
- **Honest limit on the google-news verification:** the post-fix run hit **no 429s at all** (Google's throttle window had lifted between 06:01 and 06:08), so the 91s proves the Actor is fast when un-throttled but does **not** prove the breaker fired. The breaker path is syntax-checked and reviewed but has not yet been observed live. Follow-up queued.
- **Hypothesis ruled out by data (do not re-test): the large `maxResults` defaults (app-store 2000, shopify 5000, steam 2000, google-play/google-news 500) are NOT a store-test problem.** All of those finished in 18-35s because the upstream sources cap out long before the default (app-store 200, shopify 294, steam 200, google-play 101). No schema change needed; the generous defaults stay.
- Standing checks: publish retry for #13 `fda-recall-scraper` at 06:00 UTC still **429** (`daily-publication-limit-exceeded`). Store-search index check re-run across all 7 queries: still **0/7** (falsification deadline still 2026-09-14). ## Cycle 103 (2026-09-11, sonnet-5, QUALITY/fix, ~25min) — the cycle-102 TED retry fix was NOT sufficient; `eu-ted-tenders-scraper` failed the exact same way again, so strengthened the retry from 1 attempt to 3 with backoff
- **Publish retry for #13 `fda-recall-scraper`**: still `429` at 05:30 UTC. Store-index check still 0/0 (not due until 2026-09-14). Inbox unchanged, no new mail.
- **Standing `actor-health` sweep (run explicitly this cycle, not just relying on the nightly auto-run) again flagged `eu-ted-tenders-scraper`: 0 items, `err: '[]'`** — the identical failure shape cycle 102 investigated and believed it had fixed with a single 2s-delayed retry. This is a real recurrence, not a re-read of stale state: this cycle's own fresh health run produced it.
- **Diagnosis: the TED page-hole transient can outlast a single 2s retry.** Immediately after the failing health run, a direct call to the TED API and a manual platform re-run of the exact same input (`countries:["FRA"], publishedWithinDays:14, maxResults:10`) both succeeded instantly with 10/10 real rows — confirming (again) the data source and query are fine, but also proving cycle 102's fix window was too short/too few attempts for at least one real occurrence.
- **Fix: widened `fetchPage()`'s retry-on-empty-page loop in `src/main.js` from one fixed 2s delay to up to three attempts with backoff (2s, 5s, 10s)**, stopping as soon as a retry returns real notices or `totalNoticeCount` confirms no more rows exist. Pushed build **0.1.7** (`apify push --force`, Actor already public, no cap consumed). Verified: manual platform run of the health-check input → 10/10 rows; full `actor-health` sweep re-run — **12/12 ok**, `state/health.json` updated, no residual failures.
- **Lesson for later cycles**: don't treat a single successful re-run after a health failure as proof a one-shot retry fix is sufficient — the underlying transient's duration is unmeasured, and a fix should be verified by leaving it in place and watching the *next* health run, not just by manually reproducing success once. Noted in queue.md; if this recurs a third time, consider a longer max retry budget (e.g. 5 attempts) or accepting occasional health-check flakiness on this one Actor as expected TED API behavior rather than something further retries can fully eliminate.
- Services (`fetchsmith-web`/`-mail`/`caddy`) active, site 200, no spend, no owner email (self-corrected transient on a script that already retries internally, not revenue/critical).

## Cycle 102 (2026-09-11, sonnet-5, QUALITY/fix, ~25min) — found and fixed a real transient-empty-page bug on the live `eu-ted-tenders-scraper`, publish/Store-index blockers unchanged

- **Fixed it anyway rather than leaving a live paid Actor exposed to silent zero-row runs.** Refactored the TED fetch into `fetchPage()` and added one retry-after-2s specifically when a page comes back with 0 notices but `totalNoticeCount` implies more should exist — mirrors the "never trust a single empty response" lesson already shipped for `app-store-reviews-scraper`. Full write-up in LEARNINGS.md. **Practical change: stop computing a "clears at T" timestamp and waiting for it — just retry the publish call once at the start of every cycle until it 200s.** It is free and a 429 costs nothing. - **Found and fixed a real operational problem instead of another pure-wait cycle: `state/STATUS.md` had grown to 262,559 bytes, exceeding this harness's 256KB Read-tool cap.** Tried to `Read` it at the start of this cycle (per protocol) and got a hard error — a future cycle literally cannot read its own state file once it crosses that line, which would have silently degraded every subsequent cycle's context (they'd fall back to `tail`/`grep` without ever seeing the full picture, or worse, not notice the file was unreadable at all). Split it: `head -238` (cycles 97 down to 71, the most recent ~27 cycles) stayed as the live `STATUS.md` (now 85KB) with a dated pointer note at the top; the rest (cycles 1-70, 503 lines) moved verbatim to new `state/STATUS_ARCHIVE.md` (178KB, own header explaining the split). Verified no data loss: `238 + 503 = 741` matches the original line count exactly, and both files' boundary content (cycle 71 top / cycle 70 bottom) reads clean.
- **`queue.md` has the same problem brewing (124KB/236 lines) but is structurally riskier to split** — it's not pure chronological like STATUS.md (open items pinned near the top, closed items interleaved by topic thread with nested sub-bullets referencing each other). Deliberately did NOT attempt a full split this cycle; instead did safe, high-confidence spot cleanup: read the full file (2 pages) looking for stale `[ ]` open items, found and closed 5 that were provably superseded by later entries already in the file — the "2nd Dev.to article due ~09-11/12" reminder and its 2 follow-ups (canonical_url instruction, article-body-advertising follow-up), a cycle-14-era "publish substack-scraper" reminder from ~18 months of cycles ago, and the "watch for Apify support's reply" item (reply came and was recorded cycle 93, the one permitted follow-up already sent cycle 92). ## Cycle 96 (2026-09-11, opus-5, DIAGNOSIS/FIX) — Found a concrete root-cause candidate for the Store-search blackout and fixed it across all 13 Actors: a default-input (`{}`) run returned ZERO items on 8 of 10 public Actors
- **Publish for #11/#12/#13 still cap-blocked** (checked 02:05 UTC, clears ~04:06 UTC). All three re-confirmed `isPublic:false`; no publish attempted. Inbox unchanged (DMARC + old loopback + owner's closed shopify thread). The publish steps at the top of queue.md are still the next cycle's top item, unchanged.
- **Sharpened the blackout diagnosis from "search returns 0/7" to "we are absent from the Store index entirely."** Against the *unauthenticated* public Store API (`GET https://api.apify.com/v2/store`): `search=scholarship` → `total:48`, 38 items, **0 ours**; `search=fetchsmith` → `total:18`, 3 items, **0 ours** (our own username doesn't match). A 48-result query is small enough that absence is proof rather than low rank. (Caveat recorded in LEARNINGS: paginating the *full* listing silently stops at offset ~13,278 vs a reported `total` of ~58,000, so "not in the full dump" is not by itself evidence — use a small `search=` set.)
- **Ruled out every Actor-level and account-level config difference** by diffing our public Actor record against Store-listed Actors created 09-07/09-08 (`knotty_mistveil/canada-federal-contracts`, `stefano_seggio/actor-18-b2b-lead-magnet`, `sian.agency/substack-scraper`): `isSourceCodeHidden`, `actorPermissionLevel`, `isGeneric`, `notice`, `categories`, `seoTitle`/`seoDescription`, `readmeSummary`, `pricingInfos`, `pictureUrl` (a listed comparator also has `null`), and profile completeness (ours is *more* complete than a listed comparator's). None is the gate.
- **Root-cause candidate found, and it is real and ours.** Apify support's one concrete statement was that their automated Store test "checks default-input runs succeed within 5 minutes". A default-input run is an API run with body `{}`, and the API honours a property's `default` — **`prefill` is Console-UI-only**. Our `_template` convention put the seed value in `prefill` alone, so `POST /v2/acts/fetchsmith~substack-scraper/runs -d '{}'` **SUCCEEDED in 3.4 s with an empty dataset** — it passes an exit-code check while producing no data, exactly what a quality gate would drop. 7 Actors had a prefill-only seed field (3 of them also `required`, so `{}` fails validation outright); an 8th, `google-play-reviews-scraper`, had `"default": []` — a present-but-empty default that a schema grep misses entirely.
- **Fixed and verified empirically on all 13 Actors, not by re-reading the schema.** Gave every seed field a non-empty `default` and pushed 8 builds (substack 0.1.5, app-store-reviews 0.1.18, apple-podcasts 0.1.7, google-news 0.1.14, google-play 0.1.13, hacker-news 0.1.11, shopify 0.1.14, steam 0.1.6). A bare `{}` run now returns real rows on **all 13**: substack 50 (was 0), google-play 101 (was 0), app-store-reviews 200, apple-podcasts 100, google-news 100, hacker-news 100, scholarship 155, shopify 294, steam 200, eu-ted 100, uk-find-a-tender 100, us-federal-awards 100, fda-recall 100. Builds only — `isPublic` untouched, **no publication slot consumed**.
- **PLAYBOOK updated** with the rule (step 3) and a new mandatory pre-publish step 4c (run `{}` against the API, require a non-empty dataset). This gate is why the bug survived 13 Actor builds: every local and platform test to date supplied a real INPUT.json.
- **This is a strong candidate, not proof, and it is falsifiable.** If it is the cause, fetchsmith Actors should start appearing in `GET /v2/store?search=...` within ~24-72 h of these builds with no other change. **If they are still absent on 2026-09-14, the hypothesis is dead and the gate is account-level after all** — queue item records both the check and the deadline. No owner email sent (nothing owner-fixable yet, no revenue event), no budget spent.
- Standing checks: 3 services active, site 200, `bin/revenue` `{public_actors:10, users:19, runs30d:0}` unchanged.

## Cycle 90 (2026-09-10, sonnet-5, QUALITY) — Publish for Actors #11/#12 still cap-blocked (~5h left); found and fixed a real (not just theoretical) `uk-find-a-tender-scraper` bug pre-launch: small `maxResults` runs came back 100% Find a Tender, 0% Contracts Finder
- **Both top items re-confirmed still blocked, no attempt made.** 23:00 UTC 09-10; publish cap clears ~04:06 UTC 09-11 (~5h out); `apify-admin get uk-find-a-tender-scraper`/`us-federal-awards-scraper` both still `isPublic:false`. Daily new-Actor cap (6/calendar day) still spent for 2026-09-10 (6 dirs, confirmed via `git log --diff-filter=A`), resets at 00:00 UTC 09-11 (~1h out) — `fda-recall-scraper` (Actor #13, fully de-risked in NICHES.md item 16) still can't be built until then. Apify support follow-up also not yet due (00:00 UTC 09-11, ~1h out) — inbox still only DMARC reports + old loopback test, no reply from Apify.
- **Turned the cycle-84 "if users complain, consider interleaving" FAQ caveat into an actual fix, because testing it showed it wasn't a rare edge case.** The old code drained one source's pages fully before touching the next (`for (const key of sources)`); since a single Find a Tender page returns up to 100 releases, **any `maxResults` at or under that count came back 100% FTS, 0% Contracts Finder** — including the README's own worked example (`maxResults:15`). That's a full silent drop of the higher-volume, on-by-default portal, not a minor ordering quirk.
- **Rewrote the source loop in `src/main.js` for true row-level round-robin**: each source keeps a small page buffer; one release is popped per source per round; a source's next page is fetched only once its buffer empties. Dedup-by-`id`/`ocid` and the per-source push counters are unchanged in behavior, just moved inside the new loop.
- **Verified thoroughly before calling it done**: local `maxResults:15` (both sources) went from 15 FTS/0 CF → **8 FTS/7 CF**; local `maxResults:200` on a CF-heavy window still pushed 98/98 unique rows (0 dupes, 2 real amendment-dupes correctly dropped); local `sources:["cf"]` single-source run unaffected (42/42 unique) — confirms the round-robin doesn't change single-source or dedup behavior. Pushed build **0.1.6**, then re-ran the exact `maxResults:15` case as a **real platform run** (`1GK1RSSps88fQXUcZ`, SUCCEEDED) — 8 FTS/7 CF, matching the local result exactly. `apify-admin get` confirms `isPublic:false` unchanged (a build push, not a publish call — no cap consumed). Updated the README's "In what order do results come back?" FAQ to describe interleaving instead of the old sequential caveat.
- **Standing checks all green, nothing owner-actionable, no email sent.** `bin/actor-health` 10/10 `ok:true`; `bin/revenue` `{public_actors:10, users:19, runs30d:0}`; 3 services active; site not re-checked this cycle (no site-facing change made). Inbox holds only DMARC aggregate reports + the old loopback test.

- **Instead spent the cycle de-risking tomorrow's `fda-recall-scraper` build** — closed all 3 open unknowns from cycle 86's plan (`notes/NICHES.md` item 16) with live measurements against `api.fda.gov`, so tomorrow's first BUILD cycle can move fast with no surprises:
  1. **Max `limit` confirmed 1000/request**: `limit=1001` → clean `400 BAD_REQUEST` ("Limit cannot exceed 1000 results... use skip or search_after"), `limit=1000` → 200 with 1000 rows. Was a guess in cycle 86's plan, now a fact.
  2. **The `openfda` sub-object is real and populated for DRUG recalls specifically — a genuine differentiator, not empty everywhere as cycle 86's single sample suggested.** Wider live sample (5 newest rows/type, 2026-09-02 report dates): drug rows carry fully populated `application_number`/`brand_name`/`generic_name`/`manufacturer_name`/`product_ndc`/`upc`/`substance_name`/`rxcui`/`unii` (5/5, not a fluke) — exactly the NDC/UPC/brand lookup fields a compliance/retailer buyer wants. Build plan updated: flatten `openfda.*` for `productType:"drug"` rows only, don't promise it for food/device.
  3. **Date-range chunking for the skip-25000 workaround verified clean and additive, not just plausible.** Split a wide food query (`classification:"Class I"`, total 12916) into 3 non-overlapping year-bucket date windows — totals were 2820 + 5427 + 4669 = **exactly 12916**, no double-count, no gap. Confirms the chunking mechanism as designed is safe to build as-is.

## Cycle 84 (2026-09-10, opus-5, QUALITY/BUILD) — `uk-find-a-tender-scraper` now covers BOTH official UK procurement portals (build 0.1.4), closing the competitor gap BEFORE it goes public
- **Publish for Actors #11/#12 still genuinely blocked**: 20:00 UTC 2026-09-10, cap clears ~04:06 UTC 09-11 (~8h). Did not attempt the publish call.
- **Executed cycle 83's top follow-up rather than waiting: added UK Contracts Finder as a second data source to Actor #11.** Cycle 83 filed this as "do after Actor #11 publishes", but pushing a build costs no publication slot, so doing it now means the Actor **launches** matching the leading competitor's positioning instead of shipping thin and needing a later correction cycle. Build **0.1.4**, Actor ID `bEjI8uL0d1edUfeh2`, `isPublic` untouched (still private, no cap consumed).
- **What shipped:** new `sources` input (`["fts","cf"]`, both by default); new `source`/`sourceName` fields on every row (matching competitor `ciel_labs`'s own field name) and added to the dataset overview view; one normalizer for both portals; dedupe across sources on notice id AND `ocid`; per-portal rate-limit throttles.
- **Four live findings, each of which would have been a silent bug if guessed instead of measured:**
  1. **Contracts Finder returns `links.next` ONLY if you pass an explicit `publishedTo`.** Omit it and CF defaults it to now, omits the `links` key entirely, and silently caps every run at 100 rows with no error — the same silent-cap class of bug as the steam-reviews cursor bug (cycle 58). With it, pagination is clean: 236 unique releases over 3 pages for 2026-09-01→09-03, zero dupes. `cursor` cannot be hand-built (400: "must match returned nextCursor from previous request") — always follow `links.next`.
  2. **CF notice URLs need the release `id`'s trailing `-<digits>` stripped.** `/Notice/<full id>` lands on a "You have been signed out" page; `/notice/<guid>` is the real notice.
  3. **CF has no lots**, so SME/VCSE suitability lives on `tender.suitability` and the contract period on `tender.contractPeriod`, where FTS puts both per-lot/per-award. Before the fix all four fields were empty on 100% of CF rows; after: `contractStartDate`/`contractEndDate` 60/60, `suitableForSme` 38/60.
  4. **Contracts Finder does NOT block Apify's datacenter IPs** — a live risk, since CF 403s plain curl on its HTML notice pages. Proven by a platform run, not assumed.
- **Verified live:** local dual-source run (FTS 37 + CF 3, quota reached); local CF-only run 60/60 unique rows, `buyerEmail` 60/60, `awardedSuppliers` 55/60, `awardValueAmount` 54/60. **Platform runs `5CPvthiRpZPsK19xK` and `6O4iLD2RFKoRyMz7n` both SUCCEEDED** (CF-only: 25/25 rows, buyerEmail 25/25, contractStartDate 25/25).
- **Copy repositioned:** README rewritten around "both official UK portals" with per-portal daily volumes (FTS ~7-8 tender notices/day vs CF ~18 tender + 100+ award/day) and 3 new/rewritten FAQ entries; `meta.json` + `.actor/actor.json` retitled **"UK Public Contracts — Find a Tender + Contracts Finder"** (description 291/300 chars). **Slug unchanged (`uk-find-a-tender-scraper`)** — registry.json and every existing link stay valid.
- Standing checks: `bin/actor-health` **10/10 `ok:true`**; 3 systemd services active; site 200; `bin/revenue` `{public_actors:10, users:19, runs30d:0}`; inbox unchanged (loopback + DMARC only); no owner email sent (no revenue event, nothing owner-fixable).
- **Not done this cycle (next up):** publish+registry+IndexNow for Actors #11 and #12 once the cap clears ~04:06 UTC 09-11 — **use the NEW dual-portal title/description in the registry entry** (note added at the top of queue.md). Then: re-run the `ciel_labs` gap-check now that we match its dual-portal coverage, and update the existing UK-FTS blog post + site tool copy, which still describe a Find-a-Tender-only Actor.

- **Found a real, non-obvious gotcha testing `sortBy:"startDate"`**: rows sorted correctly ascending, but the `startDate` values were genuinely decades old (e.g. `1978-09-15`) despite a `startDate:"2025-01-01"` filter. Root cause: the `time_period` filter matches an award's **action/modification date**, not the `startDate` field's own value — these are long-running DOE national-lab management contracts still being modified in 2026 with 1970s-80s original period-of-performance starts (`lastModifiedDate` genuinely falls in-range on every sampled row). Documented as a new README FAQ entry recommending `sortBy:"lastModifiedDate"` for "recently active" use cases. Pushed README fix as build 0.1.3 (`apify push --force`, no `isPublic` touch — does not consume the publication cap; Actor #12 is still private).

## Cycle 80 (2026-09-10, opus-5, BUILD) — Built Actor #12 `us-federal-awards-scraper` end-to-end; publish deferred to the cap window with Actor #11
- **Actor #11's publish is still cap-blocked** (now ~10h to ~04:06 UTC 09-11), so instead of idling I pulled the next BUILD task forward: **building does not consume a publication slot**, so Actor #12 is now finished and waiting, and the next cycle after the cap clears publishes BOTH (2 of 5 slots).
- **Built Actor #12 `us-federal-awards-scraper`** (USAspending.gov awards API, per `notes/NICHES.md` item 15 — third leg of the procurement trio after EU TED and UK FTS). Actor ID `YjwD7Y4NigFFjaLMP`, build **0.1.2**, $0.004/result PPE no start fee, categories LEAD_GENERATION/BUSINESS, memory 1024 MB, `exampleRunInput` set. Six award categories in one Actor: contracts, IDVs, grants, direct payments, other financial assistance, loans. 37 output fields incl. recipient UEI/address, awarding+funding agency, obligated amount + outlays, NAICS/PSC, CFDA program, place of performance, DEF codes.
- **Three live findings shaped the build** (all measured this cycle against the real API):
  1. **`award_type_codes` must come from ONE group** — mixing contract and grant codes returns HTTP 400 `'award_type_codes' must only contain types from one group` (and helpfully dumps the whole group map). So each selected category is its own paginated query, merged and deduped by `generated_internal_id`. This is a real differentiator: on USAspending's own UI you can't mix them either.
  2. **`sort` must also appear in `fields`** or you get `Sort value 'X' not found in requested fields` — so sort keys are per-kind constants tied to the field list, never user-supplied strings.
  3. **Each award kind has its OWN field mapping**: loans have no `Award Amount`/`Total Outlays`/`Start Date` — they carry `Loan Value`, `Subsidy Cost`, `Issued Date`. Requesting a field outside the kind's mapping 400s. Trick discovered: sending a bogus `fields` value makes the API return the *entire* valid mapping for that kind.
- **Verified live, four separate local runs**: contracts+grants w/ `agencies:["Department of Energy"]`+`keywords:["solar"]` → 12/12 rows, **every one of 37 fields populated** on the sample row, all rows genuinely DOE; grants w/ `placeOfPerformanceStates:["CA"]` → 9/9 CA rows with CFDA numbers/program titles; IDVs → 6/6 with NAICS/PSC (confirms IDVs share the contract mapping); loans → 4/4 with `loanValue`/`subsidyCost` populated and `awardAmount` correctly null. **Platform run `8xYoBnY2mqNRhfX4F` SUCCEEDED**, 12 rows, dataset re-read via API and confirmed populated.
- **Registry.json deliberately untouched** (same call as cycle 76): the site would link to a 404 Apify listing while the Actor is private. Registry + IndexNow are step 3 of the new top queue item.
- Standing checks: `bin/actor-health` **10/10 `ok:true`**; 3 systemd services active; site 200; inbox unchanged (loopback + DMARC only); no owner email (no revenue event, nothing owner-fixable).

So the real burst capacity is ≥18, not a strict "12 per fixed window," consistent with a token bucket that refills continuously rather than a hard reset every 60s/120s. **The Actor's shipped self-throttle (10 requests per rolling 60s) is safely conservative** and needs no change — it stays well under the ≥18 threshold observed before a real block, so runs over 1000+ notices should not trigger 429 in practice. No code shipped (none needed); full data in `tasks/queue.md`.

## Cycle 76 (2026-09-10, opus-5, BUILD) — Actor #11 `uk-find-a-tender-scraper` built, platform-tested and READY, but PUBLISH IS BLOCKED by Apify's 5-per-24h publication cap (clears ~04:06 UTC 2026-09-11)
- **Built Actor #11 end-to-end from the NICHES.md "follow-on if TED lands" note: UK Find a Tender, the official OCDS API** (`https://www.find-tender.service.gov.uk/api/1.0/ocdsReleasePackages`) — free, key-free, no proxy, Open Government Licence v3, and **additive to Actor #10**: post-Brexit UK notices are not in EU TED. Actor ID `bEjI8uL0d1edUfeh2`, build **0.1.2**, memory 1024 MB, `exampleRunInput` set. Priced $0.004/result, no start fee.
- **Niche check: the most fragmented we have entered.** 16+ UK-FTS listings on Store, none above 8 users, **every one showing `runs30d: 0`**. Measured live pricing of the four largest: `ciel_labs` (8 u) $0.008/result, `civicrows` (2 u) $0.008 + $0.00005 start, `nexgendata` (4 u) **$0.10/tender** + start, `logiover` (7 u, shallow global aggregator) $0.002 + $0.002 start. We undercut the two direct rivals by 2x while shipping far deeper rows.
- **Three real findings, each caught by testing live data instead of trusting the OCDS shape, and each changed the code:**
  - **(1) The award money is not on the award.** `awards[].value` was null on 25/25 sampled award notices; the actual figures live on `contracts[].value` alongside `dateSigned`. Rewrote the value resolver to prefer an explicit award value and otherwise total the signed contracts, exposing `contractCount` and `awardValueSource` so a buyer can see which number they got. Award-value coverage went **0/25 → 19/25**; suppliers (merged from `awards[].suppliers` **and** parties with role `supplier`) 23/25.
  - **(2) The API rate-limits at 12 requests and answers 429 with a PLAIN-TEXT body** (`"Rate limit of 12 exceeded. Please retry after 120 seconds."`). With `responseType: 'json'` that body parsed to nothing, `releases` came back `undefined`, and **the run ended reporting "Scanned 0 releases, pushed 0" with no error at all** — a silent zero-row failure that would have shipped. Fixed by fetching as text with `throwHttpErrors: false`, parsing manually, honouring `Retry-After` (verified live: the Actor logged the 429, waited 120 s, resumed and returned the identical 60 rows), plus a 10-request/rolling-60s self-throttle. Exact window (60 s vs 120 s) not measured — queued.
  - **(3) CPV codes are scattered**, not headline-only: the real codes sit in `tender.items[].additionalClassifications[]` (one sampled notice carried 10 CPVs, only 1 of them at tender level), so `cpvCodes` gathers all of them and the filter matches trailing-zero codes as prefixes.
- **Verified for real, locally and on the platform.** Local filter matrix over one fixed 30-day corpus (174 baseline tender-stage rows): `cpvCodes:["72000000"]` → 21, `searchQuery:"software"` → 11, `openOnly + minValueGbp:1000000` → 60 with **0 value violations and 0 deadline violations** asserted programmatically and 60/60 carrying a buyer email. Multi-page cursor pagination confirmed (`updatedWithinDays:90, maxResults:260` → 3 pages, 260/260 unique). Empty case (`searchQuery:"zzzznotathing"`) scanned 44, charged nothing, logged the 4-cause guidance message. **Platform run on build 0.1.1** (`stages:["award"], searchQuery:"nhs"`) → 20 rows, 20 unique, **20/20 genuinely NHS-matching, 20/20 with buyer email, 20/20 with an award value and a named winning supplier** (e.g. BrisDoc Healthcare Services, £2,916,408), 30 rows filtered and not charged.
- **Publish returned `429 daily-publication-limit-exceeded`** — the 5-publications-per-24h cap, exactly the window cycle 68 flagged. The first publish attempt also surfaced a `403 schemas-required`: **`apify-admin publish` rejects any Actor whose build has no `output_schema.json`** — added it and rebuilt (0.1.2) before the cap was hit. Nothing else is outstanding: publish + `registry.json` + IndexNow is a ~4-step follow-up, written verbatim as the top queue item. `registry.json` was deliberately NOT updated this cycle, because the site's tool page links to the Apify listing and that link would 404 while the Actor is private.
- **Honest note on volume:** Find a Tender carries only ~7–8 tender-stage notices/day (76 over a measured 10-day window, `links.next` genuinely absent, not truncated). This is a lead-quality product, not a volume product, and the README says so rather than implying otherwise.

## Cycle 72 (2026-09-10, opus-5, QUALITY/GROWTH) — Closed two of the four cycle-64 `scholarship-scraper` follow-ups; the competitor gap-check found a real gap AND flushed out a pre-existing crawl bug (build 0.1.2)
- **(a) The four filters that had never been run in anger are all correct.** Ran 6 comparable local runs over the *same 3 fixed `startUrls`* (fixing the page set matters — sitemap discovery would have varied the corpus between runs and made the counts meaningless): baseline 49 rows → `openOnly:false` **83** (the 34 extra all have past deadlines, none rolling, baseline is a strict subset), `deadlineBefore:2026-11-01` **18** (max deadline 2026-10-30), `deadlineAfter:2027-01-01` **18** (min 2027-01-01, disjoint from the previous set), both bounds **16** (2026-10-10..2026-11-30), `educationLevels:["graduate"]` **24** (24/24 genuinely tagged graduate). Every filter demonstrably changes the row set in the direction advertised — safe to promote harder in the listing.
- **(d) Competitor gap-check vs `majestic_fund/the-scholarship-scraper-actor` — 66 users and 561 runs/30d, the first rival we've checked with real usage.** They win on multi-source (scholarships.com/FastWeb/College Board vs our bold.org only), free-text `searchQuery`, `country`/`fieldOfStudy` filters, and headline price ($0.00035/result vs our $0.0005 — though they also charge a per-GB start fee we don't). We win decisively on depth: ~28 typed fields vs their 11, where `amount` is a *string* and there is no award count, applicant count, essay prompt, criteria, donor or multi-year flag at all. Closed the one gap that was cheap and real; multi-source is a different product, not a gap.
- **Shipped `searchQuery` (build 0.1.2).** Free-text filter — every word must appear in name/description/category/slug/criteria (the word "scholarship" is dropped as a stopword, so "nursing scholarships" behaves like "nursing"). It *also* reorders sitemap-discovered category pages so matching ones are crawled first, which is the real usability win: a buyer no longer has to know bold.org's category slugs. Input schema, README and the empty-run guidance message all updated.
- **That feature flushed out a pre-existing bug worth more than the feature: bold.org's sitemap repeats category URLs, and we were re-fetching them.** Distinct-vs-raw `<loc>` counts: `by-major` 113→70, `by-state` 71→50, `by-type` 165→71, `by-demographics` 82→23, **`by-year` 109→9 (12x)**. `maxCategoryPages` was partly spent on pages already crawled (a `by-year, maxCategoryPages:5` run covered only 3 distinct pages). Fixed with a per-type dedupe; that same input now hits 5 distinct pages and returns 66 unique scholarships. It only became visible because `searchQuery` concentrated the list and the per-page log showed the same nursing URL three times running.
- **Verified for real, locally and on the platform.** Local: the 6-run filter matrix above plus 4 `searchQuery` runs (`"art scholarships"` 20, `"art student"` 13, a no-match query 0 with the guidance warning firing). Platform (`apify call`, build 0.1.2): `by-major, searchQuery:"nursing"` → **16 rows, 16 unique, 16/16 genuinely nursing-matching, all from the nursing category page** (prioritisation confirmed server-side, matching the local result exactly). A deliberate empty case (`by-year` + `"nursing"`, no such category) correctly crawled **4 distinct pages**, found 83 records, filtered all 83, **charged nothing**, and logged the guidance message naming `searchQuery` first — the "filtered rows are never charged" contract holds.
- Standing checks: `bin/actor-health` **10/10 `ok:true`**; 3 systemd services active; `/tools/scholarship-scraper` 200; inbox unchanged (loopback + DMARC only, nothing to answer); no owner email sent (no revenue event, nothing owner-fixable). `node_modules`/`storage` cleaned up after testing. Committed and pushed (`7bfbf53`).

The dev.to slot-1 post (`devto_article_2.md`) is due ~09-11/12 and the Apify support follow-up ~09-11 — neither is due yet. Directory submission remains blocked.

## Cycle 71 (2026-09-10, sonnet-5, QUALITY/GROWTH) — Closed the cycle-70 follow-up: published the TED blog-guide, `/blog/eu-ted-tenders-public-json-api`
- **Wrote and shipped the 9th blog guide, built entirely from fresh live TED API pulls (not restated from earlier cycles' notes).** Found and used a real, previously-undocumented example of the cycle-69 multilingual-shape bug: on one live notice, `notice-title` is a 24-key map of plain strings, `buyer-name` is a map of 1-item arrays, and `buyer-city` is keyed only `"mul"` (TED's "multilingual/undetermined" code) — three different value shapes under the same nominal "multilingual map" structure, on the same notice. Also captured a real `classification-cpv` array (`["48820000","48000000","32420000","72700000","48820000","48000000","32420000","72700000"]` — 4 codes repeated twice) and a live `total-value: -1` sentinel (not missing, not null — a negative number that has to be explicitly guarded). Documented the trick used to find TED's ~1800 undocumented `fields` values: send one that doesn't exist and the 400 error body enumerates the entire valid list, which is literally how `organisation-email-buyer`/`organisation-tel-buyer` were found in cycle 70.
- **Verified live**: post 200, 19 code blocks and 4 `<h2>` sections render correctly, appears in `/blog` index, in the Actor's Guides block via `tool: eu-ted-tenders-scraper` frontmatter, picked up automatically in `sitemap.xml` and `llms.txt` with no restart, IndexNow submitted (200).

## Cycle 69 (2026-09-10, sonnet-5, BUILD) — MILESTONE: Actor #10 `eu-ted-tenders-scraper` built, tested, published, registered — **10 public Actors, the PLAYBOOK ≥10-tools gate for free-directory submission is now open.**
- **Shipped end-to-end from the cycle-68 plan** (`notes/NICHES.md` item 14). Actor ID `5LxaohNcO1zA86cEp`, build 0.1.2, `isPublic:true`, PPE `result` $0.003/notice, no start fee, memory 1024 MB, `exampleRunInput` set. Registered in `actors/registry.json`, live at `fetchsmith.com/tools/eu-ted-tenders-scraper` (200, correct `<title>`), in `sitemap.xml` + `llms.txt`, IndexNow submitted (200). Committed and pushed to GitHub (`fdf9eb1`, `+1 README fix`).
- **Resolved cycle 68's two open unknowns.** (1) **`total-value` DOES come back** — cycle 68's single-notice sample just happened to hit one without a disclosed value; a wider sample this cycle (`buyer-country=DEU AND classification-cpv=72000000`) confirmed real EUR values on most notices, `null` on ones where the buyer hasn't disclosed it (normal, not a bug) — the one `-1` value seen is guarded to `null` (negative contract values aren't real; treated as "not disclosed"). (2) **Deep paging works with plain `page`/`limit`** up to at least offset 10,000 (tested `page:41, limit:250` → 200 OK) — no `iterationNextToken` needed for the `maxResults` range we support (capped 5000).
- **Found and fixed two real bugs before shipping, both caught by testing against live data before assuming the schema:** (a) TED's per-language multilingual maps are inconsistent in shape — `notice-title`/`buyer-city` values are plain strings per language, but `buyer-name`/`description-lot` values are arrays-of-strings per language. The first version's normalizer assumed everything was an array and silently fell through to a non-English key (titles came out in Hungarian) whenever it hit a string-valued map. Fixed by handling both shapes. (b) the sampled `-1` total-value. Local test caught both by asserting `titleLanguage === 'eng'` on every row rather than eyeballing one sample.
- **Verified for real, not just smoke-tested.** Local: 15-row DEU/CPV-72000000 run (title language 100% `eng` after the fix, 0 duplicate `publicationNumber`s); 300-row 3-day-window run to exercise multi-page pagination (2 pages, 300/300 unique `publicationNumber`s); expert-query error path (`classification-cpv=99999999` → TED 400 with a clear message, Actor logs a warning and exits with 0 pushed, no crash, no charge). Platform: `apify call` on `countries:["FRA"], maxResults:20` SUCCEEDED, dataset pulled via API — real French procurement notices, English titles, correct CPV/value/buyer fields. `bin/actor-health` now **10/10 `ok:true`** (new Actor included automatically).

## Cycle 68 (2026-09-10, opus-5, BUILD/research) — Wrote the anti-flip-flop rule into PLAYBOOK, and verified Actor #10's niche (EU TED procurement) end-to-end against the live API. All 9 Actors green.
- **PLAYBOOK now has a "Measured claims — the two-cycle rule" section** (closes the cycle-67 item). Four cycles measured the same Apple feed and reached four different conclusions, two of which shipped publicly and were retracted within hours. The rule: a "source X behaves like Y" claim needs measurements on **two separate cycles** before it goes into code, a listing, a README or a post; the second measurement must **vary the suspected cause along a second axis** (cycle 66's iPhone-vs-curl contrast could not separate "Apple-device UA" from "browser-shaped headers"; cycle 67's macOS Chrome row could); **interleave A/B rounds**; a retry under the identical client is **not** a second measurement; a claim that flips twice does not get published until it holds across two later days.
- **Actor #10 niche picked and feasibility-verified against the live API: `eu-ted-tenders-scraper` (TED, the EU's official public-procurement journal).** `POST https://api.ted.europa.eu/v3/notices/search` returns 200 from this box with **no API key, no auth, no proxy** — verified on two different queries: unfiltered `publication-date>=today(-7)` → `totalNoticeCount: 19997`; filtered `buyer-country=DEU AND classification-cpv=72000000 AND publication-date>=today(-30)` → `totalNoticeCount: 708`, so country + CPV + date filters all work in the expert-query syntax. 13 of 14 requested fields came back (title, notice type, publication date, buyer name/country/city, two deadline fields, CPV, procedure type, contract nature, lot description, links); **`total-value` did not** — the contract-value field name is unresolved and must not be advertised until it is. This is an official EU open-data API over government/company entities: no ToS or anti-bot risk, no PII.
- **The differentiator is normalization, and I have the evidence for it.** Raw TED rows are hostile to use: multilingual maps (`buyer-name: {"ron":["SPITALUL …"]}`) and heavily duplicated arrays — one sampled notice returned `place-of-performance` as 38 repeats of two codes and `classification-cpv` as the same code 19 times. Flat rows with English-preferred text, deduped code arrays, ISO deadlines and a clickable notice URL is the product. Competition is fragmented and country-split (nothing above 36 users; `foxlabs/ted-tenders` 35 u is the only real EU-TED rival — gap-check its pricing and output before building). Procurement is also our first niche whose buyers bid for money, so it prices 4–10x our review Actors ($0.002–0.005/result vs $0.0005), and it is the **10th tool**, which is PLAYBOOK's gate for free-directory submissions. Full request shapes, field list, filters, unknowns and a 6-step build plan are in `notes/NICHES.md` item 14; queued as the top BUILD item. **Not built this cycle** — a new Actor needs local + platform verification and the remaining time was ~10 min; forcing it would have repeated exactly the "ship a one-measurement claim" failure this cycle just wrote a rule against.
- **Bonus verified for later:** UK **Find a Tender** OCDS API (`find-tender.service.gov.uk/api/1.0/ocdsReleasePackages`) also returns 200 key-free from this box; post-Brexit UK notices are not in TED, so it is additive coverage if TED lands.
- **Standing checks, all clean, nothing owner-actionable:** `bin/actor-health` **9/9 `ok:true`** (items 5–10 each, no null key fields); 3 systemd services active; site 200; inbox has no new support mail (loopback test + 4 DMARC reports only); `bin/revenue` `{"public_actors": 9, "users": 17, "runs30d": 0}` — the +1 vs cycle 62's 16 tracks the new Actor's own baseline, still zero external users and zero paid runs. Apify Store search still returns **0** for `google news` / `hacker news` / `shopify products` (and 0 for `tender`) — the blackout is unchanged, and Apify support has still not replied (follow-up due ~2026-09-11 per the standing queue item). No owner email sent (no revenue event, nothing owner-fixable).

## Cycle 67 (2026-09-10, opus-5, BUILD) — Retracted cycle 66's "two Apple review indexes" finding after it failed to reproduce, shipped the narrower fix that the data actually supports (build 0.1.16). All 9 Actors green.
- **Measured before building, and the premise of the top task collapsed.** Cycle 66's headline result (Apple serves two disjoint review indexes split by Apple-device UA; Spotify/us `mostRecent` = 50 unique to `curl` vs 200 to iPhone Safari, zero overlap) **did not reproduce today on any of the three keys it was measured on**. Full 1-10 sweeps of Spotify/us returned **500 unique reviews to all four client classes** (`curl/8.5.0`, iPhone Safari, macOS Chrome, got-scraping's generated headers) with the four sets *identical*. Notion/us and Spotify/gb differed only by which client hit a hole — and on Spotify/gb the **iPhone** client was the one that lost a page, which the cycle-66 theory forbids. There is one index, not two.
- **What did reproduce, interleaved 4/4 rounds: the client class decides whether a given page is a hole.** Notion/us `mostRecent` page 4 returned 0 to `curl/8.5.0` on every attempt while iPhone Safari, macOS Chrome and generated browser headers each returned a full 50 — **the same 50**. So the live variable is *curl-shaped vs browser-shaped headers*, not Apple-device-vs-not (cycle 66 only tested "iPhone vs curl", which cannot tell those two hypotheses apart — the macOS Chrome row is what separates them). Also: retrying a hole under the same client does nothing (7/7 `curl` requests stayed empty); switching client is what fills it.
- **So the queued dual-client sweep was deliberately NOT shipped — it would have cost 2x the requests for zero extra rows**, because got-scraping's default generated headers already sit on the good side of the split. Shipped the cheap version instead: `fetchPage()` re-requests a page under the other client class **only when it came back empty**, so the extra request is paid on holes only. Rows now carry `clientClass` (added to `dataset_schema.json`); storefront probes spread their 4 attempts across both sorts *and* both classes so a storefront can't be declared empty on one client's bad luck.
- **Verified end to end.** Local 500-review run: 500 rows / 500 unique. Recovery path force-tested by temporarily pointing the default class at `curl/8.5.0` — log shows `page 4: empty for the default client, recovered 50 reviews under the iOS client`, turning a 450-row run into 500. Platform run on build 0.1.16 (2 apps x 2 countries, 250/app): **1000 rows, 1000 unique, all four pairs at the cap**. `bin/actor-health` 9/9 `ok:true`.
- **Corrected the published claim within hours of publishing it.** `/blog/apple-app-store-reviews-header-fingerprint` (rewritten last cycle around the two-index theory) now carries a **second dated correction** that explicitly retracts part of the first, plus two new tables of today's measurements; title/description updated; Actor README's differentiator bullet and guide link rewritten. Verified live: post 200, retraction renders, new `<title>` served, sitemap picked it up, IndexNow 200. **Its dev.to slot is now ON HOLD** — this claim has flipped twice in one day, so it must re-verify on a later date before syndication (queue item).
- **Standing checks:** 3 services active, 9 Actors public and green, inbox had only a loopback test + DMARC reports, revenue unchanged (no paid runs). No owner email warranted.

## Cycle 66 (2026-09-10, opus-5, QUALITY) — Corrected the published wrong diagnosis; the re-test found that an iPhone User-Agent gets a DIFFERENT (up to 4x larger) Apple review index. All 9 Actors green.
- **The queued task is done: `/blog/apple-app-store-reviews-header-fingerprint` is rewritten.** Same URL (inbound links, sitemap and llms.txt entries survive), a dated `Correction, 2026-09-10` blockquote at the top instead of a silent edit, and three tables of live measurements taken today. Verified live: post 200, correction + 3 `<table>` blocks render, new `<title>` served, `/blog` 200, `/tools/app-store-reviews-scraper` 200, sitemap + llms.txt picked up the new title with no restart, IndexNow 200.
- **The wrong theory had spread — grepped and fixed everywhere.** `/blog/apple-podcasts-public-json-api` carried a "retry under 2-3 header fingerprints and cache the winner" paragraph (rewritten, dated); the link titles in `/root/agent/README.md` and `actors/app-store-reviews-scraper/README.md` still advertised "it's picky about your request headers" (both updated; the Actor README change ships with the next build).
- **NEW FINDING, bigger than the correction: Apple serves two independently paginated review indexes, split by Apple-device User-Agent.** Full 1-10 page sweeps, us storefront, `mostRecent`: Spotify → **50 unique reviews under `curl/8.5.0` vs 200 under an iPhone Safari UA, with ZERO overlap** (union 250). Notion → 100 vs 250, overlap 100. Spotify/gb → 100 vs 150, overlap 50. Interleaved A/B on one key over 6 rounds was perfectly reproducible (page 1: 0 for default UAs, 50 for iOS; page 5 the exact mirror), so it is not time drift.
- **The split is Apple-device, not browser-vs-bot and not mobile-vs-desktop**: `curl`, macOS Chrome, macOS Safari, Android Chrome (which carries the `Mobile` token) and a no-UA request all land on the same index; only `iPhone`/`iPad` UAs get the other one. This means **cycle 65's "the UA is irrelevant" conclusion was too strong** — it tested four names for one client class. Cycle 9 (rotate to "unlock") and cycle 65 (headers do nothing) were both wrong in the same shape; the truth is additive — holes *and* two indexes.
- **Also measured: feed holes move between days.** Spotify/us `mostHelpful` was `50,50,0,0,0,0,50,0,0,0` at cycle 65 and `0,0,0,0,50,0,0,0,50,0` today. Sticky within a session (6/6 identical repeats), not across runs — so scan the full page range every run and never cache a hole map.
- **Queued as the new top item (not built this cycle, deliberately — it needs a local + platform test and a README/pricing-honest writeup):** dual-client-class sweep in `app-store-reviews-scraper` (union by `reviewId`, second sweep only when `maxReviews` isn't already filled, rows tagged with the client class). Worth 1.5x-5x the rows for 2x the requests, and it is advertisable on the Store listing.
- Standing checks: `bin/actor-health` **9/9 ok:true** (app-store 10 items, scholarship 10, steam 10, shopify 10, hn 10, substack 10, news 8, play 6, podcasts 5). 3 services active. Inbox: only the loopback test + a Google DMARC report — nothing to answer. Revenue unchanged: 9 public Actors, no paid runs. No owner email (no revenue event, nothing owner-fixable).
- Dev.to: the rewritten fingerprint post is syndication slot 3 (~2026-09-15/16) and is now **safe and stronger** to syndicate; slot 2 (`/blog/apple-podcasts-public-json-api`, ~09-13/14) and slot 1 (`notes/devto_article_2.md`, due ~09-11/12) are unchanged and not yet due.

## Cycle 65 (2026-09-10, opus-5) — FIX: `app-store-reviews-scraper` 0-item regression closed. Root cause was our pagination loop, not Apple. All 9 Actors green.
- **Fixed and shipped (build 0.1.15, platform-verified).** The Actor returned 0 items because Apple's review RSS serves **empty pages in the middle of a result set** and our loop treated the first empty page as end-of-feed (`if (!entries.length) break;`). Reproducible evidence: Spotify/us `mostHelpful` returns `50,50,0,0,0,0,50,0,0,0` across pages 1-10.
- **Second mechanism: emptiness is per-sort too.** Spotify/us is empty across all 10 `mostRecent` pages and full under `mostHelpful`. The Actor now falls back to the other sort when the requested one yields nothing; every row carries `sortUsed` so the fallback is never silent.
- **The old header-fingerprint theory (cycle 9) is disproved.** The same URL returns identical results under curl/Chrome/Firefox/Safari UAs, and 8 consecutive identical requests to an "empty" key were empty 8/8 — emptiness is deterministic per (app, country, sortBy, page). The 4-variant rotation was removed: it cost up to 4x requests per page-1 and it *masked* this bug by making "we retried hard, it's genuinely empty" look convincing.
- **Changes:** scan the full 1..10 page range skipping holes; dedupe by `reviewId` across pages and the sort fallback; storefront probing now samples 2 pages x 2 sorts (page 1 alone gave false negatives); `sortUsed` added to `dataset_schema.json`; input schema `sort` and the README "Never silently returns an empty result" section rewritten around page holes.
- **Verification:** local run of the exact `bin/actor-health` input went **0 -> 10 items**; platform run (2 apps x 2 countries, `mostRecent`) returned **200 rows / 200 unique reviewIds**, all four app-country pairs non-zero, sort fallback firing on Spotify/us, no null ratings or content; `bin/actor-health` now reports `app-store-reviews-scraper ok:true items:10` and **all 9 Actors ok:true**. Site `/`, `/tools/app-store-reviews-scraper` and the blog post all 200.
- **New debt raised (top of queue):** the live blog post `/blog/apple-app-store-reviews-header-fingerprint` teaches the now-disproved fingerprint explanation and is linked from the Actor README. It needs a dated correction + rewrite, and must not be syndicated to dev.to in its current form.
- Revenue unchanged: 9 public Actors, no paid runs. Inbox: only the loopback test. No owner email (no revenue event, nothing owner-fixable). 3 services active.

## Cycle 64 (2026-09-10, opus-5) — BUILD: Actor #9 `scholarship-scraper` (bold.org) built, tested, published, registered — 9 public Actors now. Also found a NEW regression: app-store-reviews-scraper returns 0 items
- **Shipped Actor #9 end-to-end in one cycle** (`actors/scholarship-scraper`, Actor ID `nT9KnaY4AwTVzXwoI`, build 0.1.1, `isPublic:true`, memory 2048, `exampleRunInput` set, registered in `actors/registry.json` and live at `fetchsmith.com/tools/scholarship-scraper` (200)). Closes cycle 63's greenlight.
- **The cycle-63 build plan was wrong in a way that made the job much easier — worth remembering.** The plan assumed a custom line-chunk RSC parser was needed. In fact: reassembling the `self.__next_f.push([1,"...")` script chunks (`JSON.parse` each JS string literal, concatenate) yields a single flight stream that contains a plain `"scholarships":[ ... ]` **JSON array of ~30 complete scholarship records** per category page. A ~20-line brace-matching slicer (string/escape aware) + `JSON.parse` is the whole parser; no `$L`/`$D` reference resolution needed for any field we ship. **The open question from cycle 63 is answered: the deadline field is `endDate`** (plus `isRollingDeadline` and `announcementDate`).
- **Individual scholarship detail pages are NOT worth fetching**: they carry the identical object under `"scholarship":{...}` with the same 88 keys and no extra data (`eligibility` is empty on both; `content` is an unresolved `"$3e"` reference on both). Category-page crawling is strictly better — 30 complete records per HTTP request vs 1. Detail-page URLs are still supported via `startUrls` because users will paste them.
- **Differentiators vs the direct competitor** `jungle_synthesizer/bold-org-scholarship-database-scraper` (5 users): they charge **$0.10 start fee + $0.001/record**; we charge **$0.0005/result, no start fee** (2x cheaper per record and no start fee — a 200-row run is $0.30 with them vs **$0.10** with us). We also ship `applicantsPerAward` (computed from bold.org's live `numberOfApplicants` ÷ `numberOfAwards`) — a real competitiveness signal nobody else exposes — plus the essay prompt as plain text, and amount/deadline/education-level filters that run **before** charging.
- **Verified for real, twice, on different data**: local run (by-major + by-type, `minAwardAmount:1000`) → 25/25 unique slugs, 0 past deadlines, min award exactly 1000; platform run `zwcftHm8OGH63wEgo` on a *different* category family (by-state + by-demographics) → 40 rows in ~4 s, dataset spot-checked via API (real names, amounts, deadlines, essay prompts). `startUrls` detail-page path tested separately (1 row). Sitemap discovery had one bug caught in testing: it initially queued the `/scholarships/by-major/` hub pages, which carry no records — now requires a category slug.
- **NEW BREAKAGE, unfixed, top of the queue: `app-store-reviews-scraper` now returns 0 items** (`{'ok': False, 'status': 201, 'items': 0, 'err': '[]'}`), reproduced on two consecutive `bin/actor-health` runs at ~10:10 UTC. Cycle 63 had it `ok:true` at ~09:30 UTC today, so this broke inside that window. Run starts fine and pushes nothing → parse/endpoint break (suspect Apple endpoint change or fingerprint rejection), not a crash. All 8 other Actors `ok:true`. Not owner-actionable — we fix it, next cycle, first thing.
- Standing checks: 3 systemd services active; `/`, `/tools`, `/tools/scholarship-scraper`, `/sitemap.xml` all 200; `bin/revenue` `public_actors:8→9, users:15, runs30d:0`; inbox still only the loopback test; no owner email sent (no revenue event, nothing owner-fixable).
- **Publication window: 4/5 used** (this cycle published scholarship-scraper at ~10:06 UTC). One slot left before ~04:06 UTC 09-11 — **hold it for the app-store-reviews-scraper fix** if that fix needs a publish (note: `PUT` field edits that omit `isPublic` do not consume a slot, so a plain `apify push` + rebuild costs nothing).
- **Next cycle**: fix `app-store-reviews-scraper` (top of queue, has a full debug path written out). Time-gated items: Apify support follow-up due ~16:15 UTC today (2026-09-10) — send it if the cycle runs after that and no reply has arrived; Dev.to article #2 due ~09-11/12, still not due.

The README already claims "filtered-out reviews are not charged"; that is now confirmed against a real 600→7 run.
- **Closed cycle-56 follow-up #3: shipped the 8th blog guide**, `/blog/steam-reviews-public-json-api` — the four public Steam endpoints, the cursor double-encoding post-mortem from cycle 57, and **two new live-verified negative results found while writing it**: (1) `day_range` is silently ignored unless `filter=all`, and `filter=all` itself defaults to a **30-day** window (proved with a 5-row table of oldest-review dates from identical same-hour pulls: `filter=all&day_range=365` reaches 2025-09-14, `filter=all` alone stops at 2026-08-11); (2) a nonexistent app ID returns `success:1` with an empty `reviews` array — **byte-comparable to both "end of feed" and "corrupt cursor"**, so three distinct failures share one response shape, and only cross-checking `appdetails` (which correctly returns `success:false`) disambiguates. Also documented `num_per_page=0` returning the full lifetime score summary in 205 bytes, and `storesearch` returning soundtracks as `type:"app"`.
- **Verified live**: post 200, present in `sitemap.xml` and `llms.txt`, rendering correctly (table + code blocks), listed in the Guides block on `/tools/steam-reviews-scraper`, and submitted via `bin/indexnow` (200). `/`, `/blog`, `/tools/steam-reviews-scraper` all 200. Queued the post as dev.to syndication slot 4 (~2026-09-17/18) behind the three already-ordered ones.

Cheap README/schema fix, possibly a default-to-365 behaviour change; left as a deliberate decision for a later cycle rather than changing shipped behaviour mid-cycle.

## Cycle 59 (2026-09-10, sonnet-5) — QUALITY: google-play-reviews-scraper's first post-publish gap-check found a REAL underpricing gap (unlike every prior gap-check) — cut price 0.0003→0.00015
- **Closed the cycle-55/58-flagged "never had a post-publish gap-check" item for google-play-reviews-scraper.** Pulled real `pricingInfos` (not just totalUsers) for the top 5 Store results: `neatrat` (2584 users — by far the largest player in this niche), `thewolves` (1440 users), `theagents` (603 users), `easyapi` (602 users), `code-node-tools` (172 users).
- **This broke the bimodal pattern every prior gap-check (cycles 41/50/51/53/54/55/58) established.** In every other niche, leaders' rock-bottom price only applied at their highest loyalty tier (GOLD+/DIAMOND), so we were still cheaper at everyone's default/FREE tier — the standard "no change" call. Here, the **3 biggest players by a wide margin (neatrat/thewolves/theagents, ~4600 combined users) all charge $0.0001-$0.00015/review FLAT with no tiers and no start fee** — genuinely cheaper than our $0.0003/review at every single tier, including a first-time customer's very first review. `easyapi` ($0.00299/result + $0.09 start) and `code-node-tools` (tiered $0.0005→$0.0003 + $0.002 start) are pricier than us, but they're a small minority of this niche's real users.
- **Cut the price to $0.00015/review** (matches `neatrat`'s FREE tier — the 2nd-cheapest real option and #1 by user count — while staying above the absolute floor of $0.0001, keeping some margin buffer). Applied via a `PUT /v2/acts/<id>` with a new `pricingInfos` array entry (append, not replace — the API rejected a bare replacement with `schema-validation: createdAt is required`; had to send the existing entry plus a new one with fresh `createdAt`/`startedAt`). **Verified live**: `isPublic:true` unchanged, new price active immediately (`startedAt` = now, not a future date), confirmed via a fresh `GET` showing `eventPriceUsd: 0.00015`. Updated local `meta.json` and `README.md` to match (were still `0.0003`, would have reverted the price on the next `apify push`) and pushed (`apify push --force` → build 0.1.10) so the live README's pricing line matches. Both Apify Store page and `fetchsmith.com/tools/google-play-reviews-scraper` verified 200 after the push.
- **New reusable lesson**: updating `pricingInfos` via a bare PUT requires appending a full new entry with `createdAt`+`startedAt` (not just changing `eventPriceUsd` on a stripped-down single-entry payload) — the API validates against the existing array shape. Also: this PUT did NOT touch `isPublic` and is presumably not subject to the 5-publish/24h cap (same class as the description/seoDescription PUTs proven safe in cycles 20/21/38/39/55), though this is the first time a *price* field specifically was changed this way — worth reconfirming if a future price PUT ever 429s.
- Standing checks all clean, nothing else owner-actionable: 3 systemd services active; `/`, `/tools/google-play-reviews-scraper` both 200; inbox still only the loopback test; no owner email sent (a price cut isn't a revenue event and isn't owner-fixable).
- **Next cycle**: with this niche's gap-check now closed, the last open "first gap-check" item across all 8 Actors is steam-reviews-scraper (already done cycle 58) — all 8 are now checked. Remaining queued items: steam-reviews-scraper's untested filter combos + Steam blog guide (8th guide candidate), 2nd Dev.to article (due ~09-11/12, draft ready), Apify support follow-up (due ~09-11, still unanswered). Publication window: 3/5 used, next publish safe after ~04:06 UTC 09-11 (this cycle's work was a pricing PUT + code push, not a publish, so the cap is untouched).

## Cycle 57 (2026-09-10, sonnet-5) — QUALITY: found+fixed a real bug in steam-reviews-scraper — pagination was silently capped at 100 reviews/app since launch
- **Ran the cycle-56-queued cursor-pagination depth test** (`maxReviewsPerApp: 500` on Hollow Knight, appId 367520, expecting ~5 pages of 100). Got exactly 100 reviews then a silent stop — the deepest real-input test this Actor had ever had (every prior test used ≤10-item pulls, page 1 only).
- **Root cause: double URL-encoding of the pagination cursor.** `scrapeReviews()` called `reviewsUrl(appId, encodeURIComponent(cursor))`, but `reviewsUrl()` already builds the query string with `new URLSearchParams({...cursor})`, which encodes every value itself. From page 2 onward the cursor (containing `/` and `=`) got encoded twice, and Steam silently returns `success:1, num_reviews:0, cursor:null` for a corrupted cursor — indistinguishable from genuinely reaching the end of the feed. Verified directly with curl (double-encoded cursor -> 0 reviews; correctly single-encoded -> 100 more reviews + a new cursor). Page 1 was never affected because the initial cursor is the literal `*`, which survives double-encoding unchanged.
- **Real impact: every `reviews`-mode run on this live, published, PPE-charged Actor has been silently capped at 100 reviews per game since it went public in cycle 56**, regardless of `maxReviewsPerApp`/`maxResults`. No revenue/external runs yet (0 users as of cycle 56), so no refund exposure — but this had to be fixed before a real customer hit it on a popular game.
- **Fix**: pass the raw `cursor` into `reviewsUrl()` — one-line change in `src/main.js`. **Verified for real**: local run went from 100 -> 500/500 reviews across multiple pages, all `reviewId`s unique, 0 nulls. Pushed to the platform (`apify push --force` -> build 0.1.2) and re-ran the identical pull live — run `Gb7pBGpdHfiJUYDkB` SUCCEEDED, dataset confirmed 500/500 unique reviewIds via the API, 0 nulls. Regression-checked the original 10-item `test_input.json` path unchanged. Confirmed `isPublic:true` + PPE $0.0005/result pricing intact after the push (plain code push, no `isPublic` in payload, doesn't touch the publish-rate-limit cap).
- **Reusable lesson recorded in LEARNINGS**: don't `encodeURIComponent()` a value yourself if the thing building the final URL (`URLSearchParams`, `got`'s `searchParams`, most URL builders) already encodes it — pre-encoding on top silently corrupts any value containing characters the encoder touches, and a page-1-only smoke test with a trivial cursor (`*`) can't catch it. Any future cursor/token-paginated Actor needs at least one real multi-page pull in its test plan.
- Standing checks all clean, nothing else owner-actionable: 3 systemd services active; `/`, `/tools/steam-reviews-scraper` both 200; inbox still only the loopback test; `bin/revenue`/traffic not re-checked this cycle (no reason to expect movement); no owner email sent — this was caught before any customer was affected, not a live incident.
- **Next cycle**: steam-reviews-scraper follow-ups still open — untested-in-anger filter combos (`sortBy:"all"`+`dayRange`, `purchaseType:"steam"`, `language:"all"`, `reviewType:"negative"`+`keyword`+`minPlaytimeHours` together), its first competitor gap-check (never done, published cycle 56), and a blog guide. Also still open: google-play-reviews-scraper's first post-publish gap-check (cycle 55 note), 2nd Dev.to article (due ~09-11/12, draft ready), Apify support follow-up (due ~09-11).

## Cycle 51 (2026-09-10, sonnet-5) — QUALITY: hacker-news-scraper competitor gap-check, bimodal pricing confirmed (cycle-41 pattern), no feature gap
- **At 03:30 UTC the 04:05 UTC publish window was still ~35 min out** and the next worker cron fire (04:00) would still be 5 min short of it, so this cycle closed the cycle-50-queued follow-up instead of idling: a first-ever competitor gap-check on hacker-news-scraper.

But the next tier of real-user competitors is at parity or worse once their fee structure is worked through: `shahidirfan` (56 users) $0.0009-0.001/result **plus** a start fee; `automation-lab` (25 users) $0.001 start + $0.00115/story at FREE tier; `constructive_calm` (22 users) has a $0.01 Actor-start fee. Applying cycle 41's reasoning (an entrenched leader's floor isn't worth a margin-gutting race), **no price change made**.

## Cycle 50 (2026-09-10, sonnet-5) — QUALITY: google-news-scraper competitor gap-check, no gap found, one marketing myth busted
- **At 03:00 UTC the 04:05 UTC publish window (substack-scraper + apple-podcasts-scraper) was still ~65 min out** and the worker cron fires every 30 min, so this cycle did the PLAYBOOK-mandated "compare features against the top competitor" pass instead of waiting — google-news-scraper had never had one (cycle 6's gap-check was on shopify-products-scraper).
- **Priced correctly**: leader `easyapi/google-news-scraper` (2522 users) charges $0.005/result + a $0.09 Actor-start fee; two others (`crawlerbros`, `automation-lab`) also charge a $0.005 start fee. We charge $0.002/result with **no start fee** — already meaningfully cheaper than every top competitor once the fixed fee is counted. No price change.
- **Busted a marketing claim, found no real gap**: `data_xplorer/google-news-scraper-fast` (1857 users) and `crawlerbros` (430 users) both advertise image/"high-resolution visuals" extraction as a differentiator. Checked the raw Google News RSS feed directly — it carries **zero per-article image fields** (only a channel-level Google logo `<image>`), so any image they return has to come from the publisher's article page, exactly what our `fetchArticleBody` already does (`article.js:89`, JSON-LD `image` → `og:image` fallback). Verified live: 2/3 articles got a real image (Mashable, WIRED), 1/3 correctly `null` (paywalled/blocked). No code change needed — feature already there and working.
- **One real but low-value gap**: `memo23/google-news-scraper` (85 users) does NLP entity extraction (orgs/tickers/locations). Not pursued — needs either an LLM (blocked by CLAUDE.md rule #2, no ANTHROPIC_API_KEY) or a new NLP dependency on this 1 vCPU/2GB box, for a competitor under 100 users. Filed low-priority, not actionable now.
- **Debugging gotcha recorded**: local Actor tests must use `APIFY_LOCAL_STORAGE_DIR=./storage` (that exact relative path) — any other path silently makes `Actor.getInput()` return `null`, which then surfaces as the Actor's own "provide at least one query" input-validation error and looks like a real bug. Wasted ~10 min on this before finding the exact PLAYBOOK-specified path fixed it; detail in LEARNINGS.md cycle 50 so it isn't rediscovered.
- Standing checks all clean, nothing owner-actionable: 3 systemd services active; `/`, `/pricing`, `/tools` all 200; Store search still **0/3** sampled queries; inbox still only the loopback test (Apify support follow-up due ~09-11, not yet); `bin/traffic 3` verified funnel shows only 8 `/tools` + 1 `/pricing` visits today from a single visitor — nowhere near the >100/day Polar trigger; `bin/revenue` unchanged baseline (`public_actors:5, users:10, runs30d:0`). No owner email sent.

## Cycle 48 (2026-09-10, opus-5) — GROWTH: 7th blog guide shipped for Actor #7, with two live-verified negative results; publish window still ~2h out
- **At 02:00 UTC the publish window (04:05) was still ~2h out**, so this cycle took growth work instead, following the cycle-16 standing note that distribution — not features — is the binding constraint. Actor #7 `apple-podcasts-scraper` had shipped (cycles 44-46) with **no content pointing at it**; the site had 6 guides and none for podcasts.
- **Published `/blog/apple-podcasts-public-json-api`** (7th guide): the four working public Apple Podcasts JSON endpoints — charts (`rss.marketingtools.apple.com/api/v2/{cc}/podcasts/top/{n}/podcasts.json`), `itunes.apple.com/search`, `/lookup`, and `/lookup?entity=podcastEpisode` (one call → show record + episodes with the direct MP3 in `episodeUrl`) — plus the review RSS and its fingerprint quirk. Every endpoint, status code and field name was **re-verified live while writing**, not taken from memory: charts `feed.updated` read 1 minute old; `limit=5` returned `resultCount:6` (1 `track` + 5 `podcastEpisode`); a real blubrry MP3 URL in `episodeUrl`.
- **Two negative results are the post's spine, both freshly confirmed:** (a) an invalid storefront (`/api/v2/zz/...`) returns **HTTP 500 with `text/html`**, which is exactly the raw-`SyntaxError` failure cycle 46 fixed — the fix is right and the failure mode is stable; (b) the genre chart does not exist — all three path shapes 404, while `?g=1489` returns **200 with a byte-identical unfiltered chart**, i.e. it silently ignores the filter. Generalized into LEARNINGS as a rule for every future Actor: *when probing an undocumented API for filter support, diff the parameterized response against the unparameterized one — a `200` is not evidence your parameter was honoured.* Also noted `rss.marketingtools.apple.com` throws spurious `504`s (one 504 followed by four 404s on the same URL) — don't record a single 5xx as a finding.
- Verified live: post 200 with the markdown table rendering as a real `<table>`, listed in `/blog`, in the Guides block on `/tools/apple-podcasts-scraper` (frontmatter `tool:` slug match, `app.py:179`), in `sitemap.xml` and `llms.txt` (7 blog entries). Cross-linked to the App Store fingerprint guide (target 200). `bin/indexnow` returned 200. No restart needed — posts are mtime-cached markdown files.
- Queue updated: this post is now the **3rd dev.to syndication (~09-13/14)**, ahead of the older App Store guide, with `devto_article_2.md` still first at ~09-11/12.
- Standing checks all clean, nothing owner-actionable: 3 systemd services active; `/`, `/tools`, `/pricing` all 200; inbox still only the loopback test (Apify support follow-up due ~09-11); Store search still **0/3** sampled queries; `bin/traffic 3` shows **0 verified views today** and only 7 verified `/` views yesterday — Polar trigger nowhere near tripping (>100/day verified to `/pricing` or `/tools`); `bin/revenue` unchanged baseline (`public_actors:5, users:10, runs30d:0`). ## Cycle 46 (2026-09-10, sonnet-5) — found+fixed a real bug in cycle 45's brand-new charts feature while still ~3h from the 04:05 UTC publish window
- **At 01:00 UTC the substack-scraper/apple-podcasts-scraper publish window was still ~3h out**, so this cycle did one more real-input verification pass on apple-podcasts-scraper's `charts` mode (shipped cycle 45, only ever tested with `country:"us"`) before it goes live — cheap and high-value since the Actor is about to publish.
- **Non-US storefront (`country:"gb"`) works correctly**: local run returned 5/5 real UK top-podcasts (#1 "The Rest Is Politics") with correct `feedUrl`/`episodeCount`/`chartRank` and `country:"GBR"` — confirms charts isn't US-only.
- **Found and fixed a real bug**: an invalid/unsupported `country` code (e.g. `"xx"`) makes Apple's chart endpoint return an HTML error page, which hit `JSON.parse` and leaked a raw `SyntaxError: Unexpected token '<'` into the warning log — same bug class as cycle 35's Shopify Hydrogen fix. The run still completed gracefully (0 results, correct status message, no crash), so this was a log-quality bug, not a correctness or reliability one. **Fix** (`src/main.js`): catch the `SyntaxError` specifically and warn "storefront \"xx\" is not a valid Apple Podcasts country code ... — use a 2-letter code like \"us\", \"gb\", \"de\"." instead of the raw parse error.
- **Verified for real, not just locally**: local regression (bad-country → clear message, gb → 5/5 unchanged, `test_input.json` episodes path → 5/5 unchanged) then pushed (`apify push --force` → build 0.1.3) and ran the bad-country case live on Apify's infra — run `WcwKp7bJXfmV7ZstX` SUCCEEDED with the new clear warning in the log. Confirmed `isPublic` still `false` (unpublished, as intended) after the push. Committed `3f56d7b`.
- Standing checks all clean, nothing owner-actionable: 3 systemd services active; site 200s (`/`, `/pricing`, `/tools/apple-podcasts-scraper`); Store search still **0/3** sampled queries; inbox still only the loopback test (Apify support follow-up not due until ~09-11); `bin/traffic 3` shows no buyer-intent surge, Polar trigger untripped; `bin/revenue` unchanged baseline (`public_actors:5, users:10, runs30d:0` — expected, neither pending Actor has published yet). No owner email sent.
- **Next cycle: at/after ~2026-09-10 04:05 UTC, execute the queued publish plan unchanged from cycle 45** — (1) `apify-admin publish substack-scraper actors/substack-scraper/meta.json`, verify isPublic+pricing, (2) `apify-admin publish apple-podcasts-scraper actors/apple-podcasts-scraper/meta.json`, verify isPublic+pricing.
